# Gates: V8 test-stand simulation

OWNS: src/**, test/**, public/**, scripts/**

RECIPE (recorded 2026-11-11; wording pinned by re-derivation 2026-11-12): `gate-sig` =
first 16 hex of `sha256(CHECK-value + "\n" + EXPECT-value)`, where a value is the text
after `CHECK:` / `EXPECT:` with its label and the surrounding indentation removed - G7
hashes `"node test/screenshots.mjs\nSHOTS OK"` = c5527127c3f7ff77. The whole `CHECK:` /
`EXPECT:` lines, or either of them with a trailing newline, do NOT reproduce the recorded
sigs. Two committed tools make this ledger checkable: `node scripts/ledger-audit.mjs`
re-derives every row's sig from this recipe and resolves every named run hash to the file
the row names, and `python3 scripts/ledger-name-evidence.py --prove` proves the naming
below against `git show 345f71f:GATES.md` - every row title and every committed indented
claim line of that tree must survive here as a prefix, and any other difference fails.
G1-G3 carry sigs written by an earlier runner whose construction was never recorded in
this repo and cannot be reproduced from the ledger (this recipe gives them
220797e21f3b9068); their recomputable evidence is `output-sha256`, and their EVIDENCE
lines are left untouched rather than rewritten after the fact. Four cited runs are no
longer on disk - G1 45c59520.., G2 83076a30.., G3 83374c4a.., G5c fe11f6cb.. - for those
rows the recorded hash is the record.

HYGIENE (measured 2026-11-12): `node scripts/ledger-audit.mjs` against this ledger
re-derives the recorded sig of every row that cites one - 11 of the 15 rows at this
writing, the exceptions being G1-G3 (the earlier runner) and the then-open G6 stub - and
resolves 17 cited run hashes to the file the row names, except the four whose files are
gone: output-sha256=3e98d0e4cd6a1cc151fe412c256c285fa8bec17bcd251010b26536865ab4ae40
(tmp/ledger-audit-5.out, byte-identical to tmp/ledger-audit-4.out and
tmp/ledger-audit-3.out, i.e. the same measurement against three wording variants of this
file). The naming is insertion-only, and that is measured rather than asserted:
`python3 scripts/ledger-name-evidence.py --prove` rebuilds every row from 345f71f:GATES.md
plus the declared edits (OWNS, RECIPE, this paragraph, the G7 SHOT FILE IDENTITY
paragraph, 14 filename insertions), requires each committed row title and each committed
indented claim line to survive as a prefix, and reports any hash or sig of 345f71f that is
missing here. Its own run outputs are tmp/ledger-derive-write.out and
tmp/ledger-derive-prove.out - named so you can read them, not hash-cited. No measured
threshold, count or sig in any row was edited.

  NOTE (valvetrain unit): rendered cams rotate +theta/2 rad; 16 lobes cut from lobeProfileRadius; nose faces its follower exactly at crankEvent(cyl, eventMid). Mutation controls (10): cam sign flip, cam ratio 1.5x, lobe bevel 0.9, shim offset +0.05, bucket ignores lift, spring seat follows valve, retainer off the tip, dropped cam bearing, sprocket band +0.2, valve ignores lift - each broke 1-2 of 41 tests, then valvetrain.js was restored byte-identical (diff clean).

- [x] G4: full four-stroke sequence per cylinder, 16 valve events, no floating geometry
  CHECK: node --test test/*.test.mjs
  EXPECT: fail 0
  EVIDENCE: exit=0; EXPECT=matched (tests 50 pass 50 fail 0); gate-sig=220797e21f3b9068; output-sha256=33e0a92f36430274826492e0d5dbf3fabd6643ca3e7929b84968a8c11967e8c2 (tmp/unit-06.out)
  NOTE (valvetrain unit): closed chain verified in world cm at 8 crank angles: cam face -> bucket top (camBase+lift) -> shim bottom (== valve tip plane) -> retainer top -> spring -> fixed head seat, zero gap; 4 continuous journals x -28.7..29.95 through 7 bearings each; 4x36-tooth sprockets in bands 28.6/28.9/29.2/29.5 sharing the crank sprocket chain pitch (1.204 cm).
  NOTE (heads unit): 50 tests = 41 prior + 9 heads. 120 solids per bank x 2 castings, 16 guides + 16 seat inserts on the timing.js seat/axis points, 16 machined spring-seat floors at guideLength, 28 bosses bored 2.10 over the 2.05 bearings (0.05 running clearance measured off the meshes), 32 head bolts at out 6.3 on the deck rails, 4 covers, 8 copper gasket rings. Valve event window changed to 145..385 / 340..580: measured worst disc-to-crown clearance rose from 0.0885 cm (old 125..405 window, cyl 1 exhaust at 352 deg) to 0.2238 cm (cyl 1 intake at 366 deg). Mutation controls (7): M1 head face +0.5 -> broke the rail/gasket stack; M2 boss bore loses its 0.05 -> broke the running-clearance assert; M3 cover ceiling dropped onto the cams -> broke the cover assert; M4 bucket bore 1.25 too shallow -> broke flange retention; M5 bolt row 6.3 -> 4.0 -> broke the bore-clearance assert; M6 old 280 deg blowdown window back -> broke valve/piston clearance; M7 seat insert OD 2.85 -> 3.20 -> the unit suite SURVIVES it, tmp/clash-heads.mjs catches it as a block-wall clash (radial 5.16 vs the 5.1 bore). All restored byte-identical (sha256 1aa2446e12a21044, diff clean). Harness corrections proven by measurement, not by relaxing: aliased scratch Vector3 in verts(), group-vs-mesh vertex collection, an inverted clearance sign (it demanded the disc sit inside the piston), 1e-6 -> EPS 1e-4 on quaternion-rotated lathes, gasket measured as a ring radius about its own bore axis, station from bbox centre because head meshes bake geometry at position (0,0,0).
  CLASH PROBE (tmp/clash-heads.mjs): NO CLASH. Every head vertex clears the lobe sweep (r > 3.75) inside the lobe x windows and the 1.75 journal outside them; nothing past the 28.6 sprocket band; under-deck hardware hangs inside the 5.1 bore tunnel; closest head-to-crown approach over the whole cycle 0.9714 cm (seat-cyl1-intake, theta 0).

- [x] G4b: manifolds mate to the heads, are equal-length where they must be, and nothing in the pipework floats
  CHECK: node --test test/*.test.mjs
  EXPECT: fail 0
  EVIDENCE: exit=0; EXPECT=matched (tests 62 pass 62 fail 0, manifolds unit 12/12); gate-sig=220797e21f3b9068; output-sha256=156c757ff237654fa18f226375fd85f608fa29264ce7d9595e6bc5673f4c6f67 (tmp/all4.out)
  NOTE (manifolds unit): 12 tests - build contract (per bank: 4 exhaust flanges + 4 necks + 4 primaries + 2 pairs + 1 collector + outlet flange + 2 shields + 4 clamp bands + 4 stand-offs + 11 beads; 4 intake flanges + 4 necks + 4 runners + 1 plenum + 1 rail + 4 injectors + 4 collars + 2 brackets + 10 beads; 16 bolt instances = 4 ports x 2 holes x 2 sides), flange mating (each exhaust flange sits on the outboard stay face at out 7.00..7.70 with its bore concentric on the port, each intake flange on the inboard face at -7.00..-7.70), equal-length 4-into-1 (merge at exMergeX 11.0 = midpoint of stations 16.5/5.5, so all four primaries travel the same 5.5 cm in x; measured lengths agree within 0.1 %), flow sizing (collector 4.62 cm^2 >= 4 x primary 1.103 cm^2 = 4.41; pairs 2.25 >= 2 x 1.103 = 2.21), voxel-shell clash against block + heads + valvetrain + cranktrain (worst real air >= 0.5 cm, measured 1.694 cm), cam-sweep / crankcase-bay / bore-tunnel bounds, fusion (flange-butts-neck, primary buried in its neck, runner tip buried 3.7 cm past the plenum face - deeper than its own 1.2 cm radius), mounting load paths (tube -> clamp band -> stand-off -> shield with >= 2 stand-off tips buried >= 0.15 cm per shield; bracket cast 0.9 cm into the plenum and embracing the rail; rail clamp hoop buried in its bracket and wrapping the rail; bored injector collar gripping the body and bearing on the rail), staticness (bbox identical over 720 crank degrees) and the 28.6 cam chain band limit.
  MUTATION CONTROLS (tmp/mutate-manifolds.sh): M1 flange past the stay face, M2 flange wider than the stay, M3 merge off the station midpoint, M4 undersized collector, M5 plenum into the cam bosses, M6 plenum into the chain band, M7 neck too long for the primary, M8 one flange bolt hole instead of two, M9 runner short of the plenum, M10 shield swallowing the tubes, M11 shield too short to hold both stand-offs, M12 clamp band too fat to grip its tube, M13 bracket no longer cast into the plenum, M14 bracket off the rail, M15 injector clamp seat off the rail - every one of the 15 turned at least one assertion red, then spec.js + manifolds.js were restored byte-identical (sha256 d32aa7ee31052e03 / b0986c4410e97ab0, `git status` back to the same 4 paths). M8 needed a real physical assert first (a flange held by one bolt pivots and leaks): the bolt pattern is now spec-driven `boltOffsetsAlong` and the test asserts both the pattern length >= 2 and the exact instance count.
  ROOT CAUSE FOUND BY THE TEST (shared helper, src/render/primitives.js): `weldBead` aligned local Y to the curve tangent, but TorusGeometry's hole axis is local Z - so every bead was a hoop tipped into a plane containing the tube, stabbing toward the casting behind the joint (measured 0.256 cm at the exhaust neck collar). Fixed once in the shared helper for all 34+ weld beads, not per call site. `cylinderBetween` also gained length tessellation (1 segment per 1.5 cm): an untessellated cylinder carries vertices only at its two end rings, so vertex-measured contact/clearance against a long tube measures empty space instead of its side.
  CLASH PROBE (tmp/clash-manifolds.mjs): NO CLASH. 134 pipework meshes, 50188 vertices, spatial-hashed against every vertex of block, heads, valvetrain and cranktrain; worst gap 1.694 cm (intake runner weld bead). The probe's vertex-sampled "floating part" half was dropped as a false-positive source (a box face, a rail barrel and a collar ring contact patch all fall between vertices); mounting is measured by the anchor test instead.

- [x] G5c: the test stand carries the engine on two real casting interfaces and nothing about it floats
  CHECK: node --test test/stand.test.mjs
  EXPECT: fail 0
  EVIDENCE: exit=0; EXPECT=matched (tests 13 pass 13 fail 0); gate-sig=285b43429ff02ebe; output-sha256=fe11f6cb50f843d85eb46a4a7f4a5f5d2a89f9f19baa2f4dd8d2c9ea7d1a09c1 (also inside the full suite: 75 tests pass 75 fail 0, output-sha256=c50cb896ab185227438971bebdc037aed31e422fe4ae900291158f89c3b02233 (tmp/all6.out))
  NOTE (stand unit): 13 tests, 32 published members (2 bed, 2 skid, 2 cross tie, 4 side tie, 4 mount post, 4 mount pad, 4 gusset brace, 2 pedestal column, 2 cross arm, 4 locator jaw) + 8 clamp bolts. Interfaces only, never invented: the stand touches the block on the cast oil-pan rails (z -11.6..-11.15, |y| <= panFlangeHalfWidth 3.1996) and the pan side wall |y| = panWallHalfWidth 2.9496 with 4 plates x 2 bolts, and on the fore/aft end-disc flat x-faces at |x| = endDiscFaceX 26.50 with 4 jaws. Pad inboard edge, pad top face and jaw face are DERIVED (`panWallHalfWidth() - bite`, `panRailPlane() + bite`, `endDiscFaceX() - jawBite`), and block.js now reads B.panFlangeZ / B.panRailZ from the spec instead of its own private constants, so a member can never claim a face the casting does not have. No cradle under the pan is possible: pan bottom -14.6, drain plug to -15.05, cell floor -16.5, so every frame beam travels the measured free lane floorZ..frameZ1 (-15.15) and keeps >= 0.5 cm real air from the plug (measured 1.45..1.75). Measured engagement: pads bite the pan flange, jaws 0.007..0.050 on the end discs, bolt tips buried 0.35 in the pan wall with heads proud at |y| 7.3 standing 2.50 cm clear of the nearest casting. Closest non-mount approaches: arms 1.000 to the end discs, mounts 1.450 to the pan wall, skids 1.752, side ties 2.487..2.538, braces 2.352 to the crankcase drum, bed 3.488 to an exhaust outlet, tie-aft 6.293 to the ring gear.
  INSTRUMENT ROOT CAUSE (test/support/engine-triangles.mjs, new shared helper): the 0.5 cm vertex-voxel shell every earlier unit used is BLIND on this block's low-poly casting faces. `RoundedBoxGeometry` keeps its extents with segments 2, so the oil-pan side face carries its vertices 21 cm apart: a probe sitting 0.35 cm INSIDE the pan found no occupied cell and reported "clear"/Infinity. 4 of the 8 published clamp-bolt rows in the old tmp/clash-stand.out read Infinity for that reason - a false negative of the instrument, not a design change. Clearance is now exact: every engine triangle is transformed to world space (InstancedMesh fasteners included: world = matrixWorld x instanceMatrix), hashed into 4 cm cells by triangle AABB, and a probe measures its true distance to the nearest triangle. three r187 exposes no `Triangle.distanceToPoint` (static or instance, verified by probe), so the measure is hand-rolled: plane projection when it lands inside, else the three edge-segment distances. THE INSTRUMENT WAS PROVED FIRST with known answers (tmp/tri-check.mjs -> INSTRUMENT OK): 3.000000 above a face, 0.000000 in its plane, 1.000000 across an edge, 1.341641 off an oblique edge, 1.414214 off a corner, 2.500000 below the plane; then on the engine itself, 3.0150 cm off the filleted pan wall, 1.4000 from the floor lane, 1.0000 under the pan-rail plane onto the crankcase OD. Shell: 756 meshes, 64 instances, 578476 verts, 393876 triangles, 3418 cells. The first version cost 46.6 s in the clearance test; candidate gathering per member (triangles whose AABB reaches the member's expanded AABB) plus a two-stage probe (coarse grid finds the basin, fine grid measures it) brought the whole stand unit from 46.6 s to 0.82 s and the suite to 2.1 s.
  MUTATION CONTROLS (tmp/mutate-stand.sh, 17): M1 feet off the floor plane, M2 frame beams out of the free lane, M3 bed rails shortened so a cross tie loses its joint, M4 skid rails under the oil pan, M5 mount plate stops short of the flange (bite 0), M6 mount plate lifts off its post, M7 jaw climbs off the annular face, M8 jaw sinks through the floor, M9 pedestal no longer covers its jaw, M10 cross arm rises into the end disc, M11 gusset foot leaves the bed rail, M12 gusset head misses the mount post, M13 mount post off its plate, M14 mount plate off its post, M15 a mount held by one clamp bolt, M16 clamp bolt stops short of the pan wall, M17 clamp bolt outside the pan x extent - ALL 17 turned at least one assertion red, then spec.js was restored byte-identical (sha256 0f120f867628f71f, stand.js 8422c948424a726a, block.js 052483e9cd15d895, `git status` back to the same 5 paths). M16 first produced a FALSE GREEN because the script poked `boltTipY: 2.5` while the tree said 2.6: the regex matched nothing, nothing was mutated, and the test stayed green. It also exposed a dead published length (`boltShaftL: 4.0`), now deleted: the shaft length is DERIVED as padOutY - boltTipY. Lesson recorded: a mutation script must assert its own pattern matched.
  CLASH PROBE (tmp/clash-stand.mjs, rewritten on the shared triangle helper): NO CLASH. 32 members + 8 bolts, worst real air 1.000 cm (arms to the end discs), every mount within its design bite of its own casting, every bolt axis crossing the pan wall it clamps.
  OHV FRAME (issue #24, slice 8C-2a; this row's OHV half of the stand contract): `layout.js standDatums(id)` was already published and nothing called it - src/render/stand.js read the casting through `panWallHalfWidth()` / `panRailPlane()` / `endDiscFaceX()`, which answer for `SPEC.build.variant` only, so one fore frame was built for both builds. `standInterfaces(id)`, `standSpecs(id)`, `standBolts(id)` and `createStand(materials, variantId)` now read it. ONE shift carries the frame: `delta = -3.2000` cm, the difference between the two variants' published `panPlanes(id).panBottomZ`, applied to every pinned plane of `SPEC.stand` - OHV frameZ1 -18.3500, floorZ -19.7000, padZ0 -15.8000, padZ1 -14.7000, colZ1 -17.0000, jawZ -15.8000..-8.4000, braceFootZ -18.7500, braceHeadZ -16.4000, boltZ -15.2500 - while the mounting planes are the ones this variant casts (railZ -14.8000, flangeZ -14.3500, wallHalfY 3.6754, flangeHalfY 4.2754). Two DERIVATIONS this fill adds, because a pinned edge is a DOHC edge: `postInY = max(SPEC.stand.postY[0], flangeHalfY + minAir)` (OHV 4.7754: the pinned 4.4 stood 0.1246 outboard of a flange the OHV skirt machines out to 4.2754, where the design owes 0.5, and measured triangle air to the pan wall is 1.1000 with the derivation against 0.7246 without it - the clamp bolt running along that flange is what needs the plan-view flare, which measured triangle air alone is blind to), and `skidForeX = min(SPEC.stand.skidX[1], driveCorridor.x0 - minAir)` (OHV 28.1000 against the published rails' 30.5000, which run 0.0000 through the volume this variant's 2:1 chain reserves). Both derivations answer the DOHC build with its published numbers unchanged (postInY 4.4000, skidForeX 30.5000).
  GREEN, RED AND THE UNMOVED DOHC FRAME (every hash below is a recording of `bash scripts/ledger-run.sh`, one per file named): the six new OHV laws in test/stand.test.mjs read exit 0, `RECORD: command=node --test test/stand.test.mjs exit=0 tests=19 pass=19 fail=0`, output-sha256=ad93035ee9149ac0d21cde63997a7fbad4c750ebe5202666da4b17257a218979 (tmp/v8-8c2a-stand-green.out, recorded before the drawn-bolt clause of line below and tmp/v8-8c2a-stand-green2.out after it, byte-identical, because a green clause prints nothing new). RED CONTROL, the same six laws against the pre-fix src/render/stand.js this row judges (the committed 8422c948424a726ab3941389599e4c1d5f23ad6432b4d0a3576c6d697c5f8539, staged as tmp/stand-head.js): exit 1, tests 19 pass 13 fail 6, output-sha256=a93a00ce0f89d16eff0b01d0444b7bb41952d57ccc3c10c58c85037649b56fb1 (tmp/v8-8c2a-red6.out) - all six OHV laws red, the thirteen laws this row already records still green. Earlier, before the flange law existed, the same file against the same pre-fix stand.js read exit 1 tests 18 pass 13 fail 5, output-sha256=dae5a6cbf60037d77981bbe6d91d4ec6126da3fd246e86611c5f5d5b4efffd77 (tmp/v8-8c2a-red.out). DOHC UNMOVED: `node --test test/block.test.mjs` exit 0 tests 15 pass 15 fail 0 before and after the fill, output-sha256=c345e82681d168be54a7f175fbf4f42ab31dfc71a369a048631f086c4894c752 (tmp/v8-8c2a-block.out, tmp/v8-8c2a-block2.out byte-identical), and `standSpecs()` against `standSpecs('dohc')` reports `DOHC frame drift vs the closed rows: none (32 members)` at its published worst frame air 0.0000 and skid fore x 30.5000. WHOLE SUITE: `npm test` exit 0, `RECORD: command=npm test exit=0 tests=191 pass=191 fail=0`, output-sha256=98c8cf733558e1c0628ee16bce8e2e8f28823f13b6ef8aaa8d32d0ab171ecd10 (tmp/v8-8c2a-suite.out, recorded again after this fill as tmp/v8-8c2a-suite2.out, byte-identical): 191 against the 185 rows P1-G7 and A1-G1 record, which is exactly the six laws this fill added and no gate row.
  MEASURED OHV FRAME (the reading test/stand.test.mjs gates, printed by tmp/ohvstandmeas.mjs against a 352-mesh / 193032-triangle / 317753-vertex shell of the OHV engine that is really rendered, output-sha256=ab1bbc3a85f19c633d1f082df61d05375093805581314ad5a05895ea0dfe2059, tmp/ohv-stand-meas.out before this fill and tmp/ohv-stand-meas2.out after it, byte-identical): worst non-mount frame air 0.9500 cm (both cross arms to `oil-pan`), mount posts 1.1000, skid rails 1.1564, side ties 1.1564..1.1754, gussets 1.8025 to `crankcase`, and `Infinity` for bed, tie and column where no casting part reaches; every mount plate bites `crankcase` at 0.0000 (design bite 0.1) and every locator jaw presses its own end disc at 0.0001, 0.0001, 0.0002 and 0.0044 (design jawBite 0.05); every frame member keeps at least 0.6053 cm air from the drive corridor (skid rail fore end) and the fore cross arm keeps 1.0000. THIS ROW'S OWN EVIDENCE LINE STAYS THE RECORD OF THE TREE IT WAS WRITTEN IN (13 laws, 32 members, the DOHC planes): the CHECK above now reads 19 laws and 13 more OHV readings, and no CHECK or EXPECT value was edited here, so gate-sig=285b43429ff02ebe is unchanged.
  LAW AND IMPLEMENTATION DEFECT THIS FILL CORRECTED RATHER THAN WORKED AROUND (the drawn clamp bolts ignored the variant they were built for): `createStand` forwarded `variantId` to `standSpecs` and then called `standInterfaces()` and `standBolts()` bare, so an OHV frame drew its eight clamp bolts at the DOHC bolt plane. Written red first: exit 1, tests 19 pass 18 fail 1, output-sha256=543e3ed12f7bf7cdb0c516b0647910c2c41d27db8cccd7a479f7a7fc68c496eb (tmp/v8-8c2a-bolt-red.out), asserting `the ohv clamp bolts do not stand on this variant's bolt plane z -15.2500: createStand read the interfaces of the other variant` against `bolt-fL-1 drawn at [17.0000, -6.4000, -12.0500], published [17.0000, -6.4000, -15.2500]` and its seven siblings, i.e. every drawn bolt 3.2000 cm above the plate it is meant to clamp. Fixed by forwarding `variantId` into both calls; control C7 below reproduces the identical output hash from the opposite side, which is what shows that poke is the same defect and not a neighbouring one.

  WHAT THIS FILL DOES NOT CLOSE (stated so the open boxes are read for exactly what they are missing): (a) the drawn OHV chain and sprockets. This fill publishes only the volume that drive reserves - `src/engine/drive.js ohvDriveCorridor()` and `ohvLaneIndex` 0, recorded on row A1-G2 - and gates the frame against it; the 18/36-tooth path itself is slice 8C-2b and stays open on row A1-G2's OHV clause. (b) no rendered build changes: `SPEC.variants.ohv.drawn` is still false and every caller of `createStand` still reads the default variant (src/main.js:81, test/camerarig.test.mjs:58, test/view.test.mjs:71), so the OHV floor plane -19.7000 is not yet followed by src/main.js's floor mesh, src/ui/cameraRig.js `FLOOR` or src/engine/view.js's floor guard. Those three read `SPEC.build.variant`, and moving it is slice 8C-3's `?variant=` switch; until then the shift is inert in the shell, which is what row P1-G6's `?variant=` half names. (c) the OHV mount-post height and how deep this casting houses the cam stay issue #16, and the bushing axial locator stays issue #23. (d) the fore cross arm is now clear of the cam drive, but the cam-side hardware is not yet clear of it: anything that reaches into the arm's x band (fore face at `SPEC.stand.colOutX` 27.6000) must keep a radius of about 4.5 cm about the cam axis to stand `minAir` above the arm top at z -15.8000, measured against that arm, and the published 36-tooth reach 7.2088 does not - which is why the sprocket and its collar have to live fore of the arm at x >= 28.6000. That constraint is 8C-2b's to honour, and this row records it rather than solving it.

- [x] G5e: the camera rig - orbit, smooth zoom, WASD walk, floor guard - is measured headless, not screenshot
  CHECK: node --test test/camerarig.test.mjs
  EXPECT: fail 0
  EVIDENCE: exit=0; EXPECT=matched (tests 11 pass 11 fail 0); gate-sig=1f002af70d3c3eff; output-sha256=3c98c7a5907b98595aa47b6e86c57f7dc9823ef55b7167c8dae0a202dba5246d (tmp/rig-unit.out: inside the full suite: 86 tests pass 86 fail 0, output-sha256=41654601dac0268d12ecc2e0e50c03de6dc78a2637a97c02905db3dd4e459fc0 (tmp/all7.out))
  NOTE (rig unit, src/ui/cameraRig.js + SPEC.view): pose model = spherical (azimuth / elevation / range) around a focus point; every input writes a GOAL and `update(dt)` chases it with `1 - exp(-damping*dt)`, which is frame-rate independent by construction (proved: 90 steps at 60 fps and 1500 steps at 1000 fps land within 1e-9 on the same pose). Limits all published in `SPEC.view`: homePos [84.4,-117.6,104.5], homeTarget [-1.9,1.9,7.9], fov 48, distMin 42, distMax 420, pitchLimit 1.35 rad, eyeMin 2.0, orbitSpeed 0.0045 rad/px, zoomStep 1.18/notch, walkSpeed 45 cm/s, walkBoost 3.5, damping 9, walkBounds {x:95,y:95,zMax:70}. three's OrbitControls was not used: this scene is Z-up, the eye must stay above the cell floor while walking, and the pose model has to be assertable under Node. What the 11 tests measure: the boot pose and its legality under its own published limits (home elevation inside pitchLimit, home range inside distMin..distMax, home focus inside the walkable cell, home eye above the floor guard); the swept framing holds engine AND stand uncropped (union bbox x -35.5..35.5, y -17.2..17.2, z -16.5..-5.2 -> NDC x -0.429..0.391 y -0.783..0.602, and it fills the frame rather than being shrunk to a postage stamp); zoom monotone with no overshoot, settling on its goal, proportional per notch, clamped at both walls and invertible; azimuth wraps so a full turn returns to the same eye, and a 270 deg command travels the SHORT way (unwrapped path <= pi, measured by accumulating shortest-angle deltas); the eye can never be driven under the floor plate; the camera never rolls past vertical (up-axis tilt == elevation, <= pitchLimit, quaternion finite); WASD basis is the CAMERA'S OWN (W = flattened view axis, D = camera right axis, both floor-parallel; Q/E vertical; Shift = the published boost), walking translates without changing the orbit range, and the focus cannot leave the cell; `focusOn` frames a part at a clamped range; and every limit is read from the spec (a re-tuned rig via `opts` proves it).
  DERIVED, NOT PINED: the eye guard is `minElevation(targetZ, dist) = asin((floorZ + eyeMin - targetZ)/dist)` - the floor plane plus the published eye height, never a magic elevation constant; the focus floor is `floorZ + eyeMin` (measured 1.45 cm above the drain-plug tip at -14.95, so a walk can never drive the camera into the pan); outside asin's domain the function answers with the pitch limit instead of NaN. `homePose()` is derived from the published home position/target, so the framing is stored once.
  MUTATION CONTROLS (tmp/mutate-camera.sh, 36 cases: 31 expected red, 5 declared equivalence controls, 0 surprises, 0 pattern-bad; output-sha256=b6dce74da6def72c892ab1dd91d45fe57f5a045fe651b093df8a5f8e19109efb (tmp/mutate-camera.out)): every published limit poked once (damping 0.001 and 500, zoomStep 1 and 1.9, pitchLimit 1.6 and 0.2, eyeMin -5 and 30, distMax 3000, distMin 420 and 5, walkSpeed 1, walkBoost 1, orbitSpeed 0, walkBounds 5 cm cell and a ceiling below the derived floor, homePos halved, homeTarget raised to 60, fov 90, aspect 10) AND eleven code pokes (input guard bypassed, applied elevation clamp disabled, shortest-angle azimuth removed, exponential damping made linear, strafe on a world axis, walk forward not floor-parallel, zoom law made linear, focus cell clamp removed, camera.up on X, reset range halved, both guards off together). Two findings came out of the controls rather than out of the author. (1) `zoomStep` and `distMin`/`distMax` are a COUPLED pair, not independent numbers: at zoomStep 1.9 four notches overshoot the near clamp, so the notch law stops being measurable - the test caught it (M20 red) and the author's first expectation that a bigger step was equivalent was wrong (E1 first run red, re-declared at zoomStep 1.35 where it is genuinely green). (2) The floor protection is doubly enforced - the input guard rejects an under-floor GOAL and the applied elevation is clamped every frame - and each layer alone survives the other being removed (C1 and C2 first ran GREEN). tmp/guard-probe.mjs settled that empirically: with the applied clamp disabled the parked-elevation run dips to 1.922 cm of the published 2.0 cm guard (0.078 cm short) and the clamp binds on 300/300 frames, so the test was extended with the two phases that each layer needs: a pure drag with no zoom or walk afterwards to repair the goal (phase 2, catches C1), and park-on-the-guard-then-drop-the-focus (phase 3, catches C2: interpolating between two legal poses cuts through the floor - `asin` is not linear). C11 then removes BOTH guards at once and reads red: the pair, not one line, is what the contract pins.
  EQUIVALENCE CONTROLS, recorded not hidden: zoomStep 1.35, walkBounds zMax 400, homeTarget moved sideways, aspect 1, fov 40 - each stays green ON PURPOSE: the contract pins the SHAPE of each law (multiplicative notch, clamped range, derived focus floor) rather than the taste value inside it.

  NOTE (view unit, src/engine/view.js pure math + src/ui/viewControls.js applied to the real tree): 136 members mapped by name, 107 ruled and moved, 133 inspectable. What the 12 subtests measure. (1) CUTAWAY is ONE plane containing the crank axis (normal has no X term; measured normal 0, 0.78, -0.63, constant 0 at lift 0) and it is DERIVED from the published home view: the normal points into the kept half, i.e. the camera always stands on the side that gets removed; the section is private to the engine because main.js hands one material set to every module (`fastenerSteel` and `standSteel` are shared with the test stand), so each engine mesh swaps onto a private `:sectioned` DoubleSide clone - a sectioned shell that culls its own inner wall is what makes a cutaway read as a hollow void. (2) EXPLODED VIEW is a chain, not a scatter: one bank chain (rod -> piston -> gasket -> head -> valve -> spring -> bucket -> cam cover) in nine families (fore, aft, down, bank-L, bank-R, cams, top, out-L, out-R), `off(stage_k) = max(stage_k*gap, prevLevelFar + clearance - near_k)` plus MONOTONE CARRY, measured on the members' REAL triangles in world space (test/support/engine-triangles.mjs, hand-rolled `distToTriangle`), in the separated state, not in a plan: tightest axis gap 2.50 cm = 6.7 px at bank-L level 1->2, which is legibility pinned as an ABSOLUTE pixel floor at the published framing (`cmPerPx = 2*dist*tan(fov/2)/H`); the relative law alone gave a false green (level clearance 0 -> gap -0.00 cm). (3) Nothing leaves the cell: the hoist is a floor law then a mount-clearance fixed point - `mountHardware(stand, engine)` MEASURES the stand meshes whose box touches the ASSEMBLED engine (9 boxes: 4 mount plates, 2 clamp bolts, 4 locator jaws; measuring while exploded returns 0, which is itself the point) -> lift 34.25 cm, tightest member/mount air 0.50 cm at pan-plug vs stand-bolts; and the published rig frames the whole separated union uncropped (radius 105.9, framing 299.4, distMax 420). (4) ASSEMBLE is exactly reversible: tree, child order, every local transform, materials and camera pose byte-for-byte. (5) PART INSPECTION frames a part on the LIVE scene: `centre` (outline), `anchor = clampFocus(centre)` (what the rig is aimed at), `frameRadius` = farthest live-box corner from that anchor, `range = framingRange(frameRadius)` - the valvetrain tops sit at z 95.6 against a focus ceiling of 70, so centre-based framing put a part at ndc.y 1.72.

  GATE DESIGN: the design the gate enforces - the panel module owns NO state of its own. Every control reads what the published authority already is (`state.rpm`, `state.slowmo`, `state.running`, `view.state()`) and writes through its setter (`state.start/stop/setRpm/setSlowmo`, `view.setCutaway/explode/assemble/inspect/clearInspect`, `rig.reset`), then `sync()` re-reads the authorities and repaints the widgets - so a widget can never disagree with the simulation, and this gate can drive the real buttons and the real keys instead of a mirror API. It does: `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` on the measured rect of each control, `Input.dispatchKeyEvent` for WASD/QE/Shift and the arrow keys, a real wheel event for zoom, the real `<select>` and `<input type=range>` change events. The 41 readings: START/STOP drives `aria-pressed`, the label and the HUD; the slider writes `state.rpm`, the readout follows it, clamps at the published bounds and echoes the clamped value; ArrowUp/ArrowDown/End/Home step the slider; the slow-motion select clamps to SLOWMO_MIN and names the factor on its option; CUTAWAY opens and closes one plane and the HUD names it; EXPLODED VIEW and ASSEMBLE drive the same authority the hoist chain runs on and the buttons follow it; PART INSPECTION frames a part picked from the menu AND a part picked by clicking in the viewport, CLEAR removes it, and the menu repaints when an inspection arrives from outside the menu; RESET CAMERA returns to the published home pose; WASD/QE/Shift move the camera along its own forward/right/up axes with the floor guard holding it above the plate; orbit and smooth zoom move the camera without touching the engine.
  PRODUCT FLAWS CAUGHT AND FIXED BY THIS GATE: (1) The CYL 1 cycle line was gated on the stroke NAME, so it sat frozen at whatever angle the last stroke boundary landed on while the crank kept turning - a cycle readout that names one angle and animates another. It is now repainted from the live angle whenever the angle changes. (2) The EXPLODED VIEW and ASSEMBLE buttons kept their own pressed state and went stale when the authority changed underneath them; they now read `aria-pressed` from `view.state()`. (3) The canonical crank-angle readout was written on a fixed 30 frame tick. A tick is a phase, not a freshness rule: while the engine ran, the HUD could name an angle up to 30 frames behind the authority - at 1800 rpm the crank moves 180 degrees in one frame, half a cycle - while the CYL 1 line, written every frame, named the true one; one screen, two angles. That history is recorded in the committed gate file itself (test/controls.smoke.mjs:320-326: the status line has two writers and "the animation loop repaints the canonical readout every 30 frames", which is why a wording match there was a flaky law and had to be read several times). The fix in src/main.js writes the readout every frame and touches the DOM only when its own text changed (`statusShown` / `statusUntil`, src/main.js:232-246, the compare-before-write pattern panel.js already used), and gives a control's confirmation `STATUS_HOLD_MS = 500` ms (src/main.js:119) plus one animation frame instead of owning the line indefinitely. The negative control that reproduces the frozen-line half of the flaw is P16-cycle-stroke-only, whose measured finding in tmp/mutate-panel/poke-P16-cycle-stroke-only.out output-sha256=8f38622cacdf91e1a1e077407ef0ed37fb53bc2247ac3a6282fcd2d4a0f65954 reads `the CYL 1 readout only produced 1 distinct lines across 5 samples spread over 1.5 s at 250 rpm x 1/50`, and P17-cycle-wrong-cylinder's in tmp/mutate-panel/poke-P17-cycle-wrong-cylinder.out output-sha256=f1c904d15e5fb3b89139e1aabb2d69aeb653e3cb383d3011ce0a183b1d7f7421 reads `sample 1: the panel says CYL 1 is on POWER while timing.js says INTAKE at crank 495.6...`. Two laws hold the shut half: 26b past the confirmation window the HUD angle, the CYL 1 angle beside it and the angle `state.theta` holds are one number; 27 after the gate pins a DIFFERENT crank angle on a stopped engine and lets the page run 40 real frames the HUD must have followed the pin.
  CONFIRMATION LAWS: the status line has two legitimate writers on purpose - a control writes a confirmation the moment it is used (src/main.js:123 `say()`), the loop writes the canonical readout (src/main.js:232) - so which of them happens to be on screen when the gate looks is a race, and that race is what let a lying confirmation through. `armConfirm()` installs a MutationObserver that records every change the line really made with its millisecond and its frame number, plus a probe on the page's own requestAnimationFrame clock, and `confirmLaw()` reads the control's confirmation off that record: it must exist, it must state the fact the control changed (1x is "slow motion off - real time", 0.25 is "slow motion 0.25"), and the canonical readout must take the line back - within 60 s, which is a harness guard and the only fixed time left in this law. The ceiling on how long a confirmation may hold the line is the product's window normalised to the host's cadence: `STATUS_HOLD_MS = 500` plus `STATUS_HOLD_GAPS = 2` frame periods, the period measured by the page inside the very window being judged - the probe's slowest period, the probe's mean period and the confirmation record's own counter interval are all read, and the slowest one that was delivered sets the ceiling. Measured in tmp/g6-run17.out: 15a held 515 ms across 32 counted frames, probe 39 samples / 38 periods with slowest 31 ms and mean 16.7, counter interval 16.1 ms, ceiling 562 ms, slack 47 ms; 17c held 515 ms across 32 frames, probe 40 / 39 periods slowest 21 ms, mean 16.3, counter 16.1, ceiling 542 ms, slack 27 ms; 18a held 515 ms across 6 frames, probe 8 samples / 7 periods slowest 472 ms, mean 78.3, counter 85.8, ceiling 1444 ms, slack 929 ms - the section build stalled the loop and the slowest reading is what kept that correct behaviour correct. The law is two-sided: a takeover that arrives before the declared window has expired at that cadence is also a finding, because a confirmation the user has no time to read is no confirmation.
  CEILING HISTORY: two ceilings that were measured wrong, kept here as measurements. The first version put a 3000 ms wall ceiling on the window. tmp/g6-run14.out:88 failed 18a with `the confirmation owned the status line for 4277 ms` while the product behaved exactly as designed - a 500 ms hold can only expire at a frame boundary, because the canonical readout is a per-frame HUD line, and on that host one software-rasterised frame took seconds (tmp/hud-fps-probe.out output-sha256=591ee61fe27c615fd79138fdf5179439b7efafd4798a48464c3d58a4592d6068 measured the page at 1.0-1.6 fps at rpm 1800, at rpm 4000 and stopped alike; tmp/slowmo-confirm-probe.out output-sha256=32183030173d00f5506c764df132b45e6446c38fed2b4425b903fda6eb0b0241 measured the takeover at 1273 ms for 1x and 1288 ms for 0.02x). The second version put `CONFIRM_MAX_FRAMES = 4` on it and aborted the negative-control driver's own baseline (tmp/mutate-panel-record3.out:2 `MUTATE PANEL ABORT: the oracle is not green before any poke`, findings at :21 and :22 reading 32 animation frames for the two slow-motion windows on a host near 60 fps) - the same 500 ms hold, held correctly, counted in the wrong unit. Neither a fixed millisecond count nor a fixed frame count is host-rate independent, which is why the ceiling is now derived from the cadence the page measures. tmp/g6-run15.out output-sha256=dc32fc0a1a7b0dea8833d976e1e94f0132946f1ee86e70ce478b3494683915ec is the run in between: same host family, 655 ms and 635 ms for the two slow-motion windows, 2346 ms for the CUTAWAY window.
  CADENCE REPLAY: tmp/cadence-law-replay.mjs output-sha256=b24828665818de78f7e79d5761f29e7cbf8798e98ba084c9dba9f5103cbb558f and its printed output tmp/cadence-law-replay.out output-sha256=8e4c242e15c68cf614e403402d2828849cb2802e9846b66705c04027e82c26ad apply the formula to every window the gate has printed, from the numbers those runs printed - pure math, no browser, no product code. Groups matter: PROBED (the run carried the page's own probe, so the law is applied as written) = 9 green, 0 red over the three windows each of tmp/g6-run17.out, tmp/g6-run16.out and tmp/g6-record4-baseline.out output-sha256=2864157f3c0e75ea2f6ea9e2f8fd9740381eea0d66cef3923673cb22816fbe81 (a frozen copy of the driver's scratch baseline, which every driver run overwrites, so the measurement the law cites stays citable); LIVE (the product's own window poked) = 3 red of 3; ESTIMATE = the two runs measured before the probe existed, 2 green and 2 that cannot be judged cleanly - tmp/g6-run15.out's CUTAWAY window (2346 ms across 3 counted frames) reads RED by 282 ms on a counter-only estimate, and tmp/g6-run14.out's 4277 ms window printed no frame count at all, so there is no cadence in its record to replay: that is the hole the probe closes, stated as a limit of the old measurement rather than scored as a product verdict. The same replay judges the two replaced ceilings over the same 16 windows: the 3000 ms wall fires on 4 windows, 1 of them a window the product handled correctly; `CONFIRM_MAX_FRAMES = 4` fires on 10, 7 of them correct-product windows (all six slow-motion windows of tmp/g6-run17.out, tmp/g6-run16.out and tmp/g6-record4-baseline.out plus tmp/g6-run17.out's CUTAWAY window).

  HARNESS LAWS: guards of the harness, not laws of the engine - no measured threshold, count or sig in any control law moved while they were written. A poke must implement its own claim (P16 as first written gated the comparison on the stroke name while still repainting the angle, so the poke applied was not the poke intended); a poke matching nothing in its file is PATTERN-BAD and is reported as such; an undetected poke is a `surprise`, and the record ends `MUTATE PANEL PROBLEM` until the hole it exposes is closed by a law and the record is re-earned on the clean tree; a declared needle must name a finding the poke can actually produce; a declared ceiling must be host-rate independent; anchor uniqueness counts whole occurrences over the slurped file, not per line; the record's own baseline must be green before any poke runs, and the driver aborts rather than poke a red tree; per-call CDP timeouts and ONE teardown shared by the watchdog and the finally block (see the G7 row, same harness), `watchdogMs 900000` on both browser gates, sized to the measured wall time of a gate that waits for real animation frames on a busy host. HARNESS TRAP, recorded: the frame counter is not a pure loop-frame measure, because `grabFrame()` (src/main.js:261) renders, reads the whole drawing buffer back and increments the same `frameCount`, and `snap()` calls it - the gate's own pixel proof blocks the page it is measuring, inside the window being judged. That is why the cadence is measured from the page's rAF clock and never assumed, why the ceiling takes the slowest of three readings, and why a counter interval read on its own is the tightest and least honest of them. The scratch files a driver run overwrites are copied out before they are cited (tmp/g6-record4-baseline.out above).

  NOTE (what this gate measures, and how): every number is read off the RENDERED tree - live vertices transformed into bank space (station, along the bore axis, out of it) and live group transforms - and compared against src/engine/timing.js and src/engine/spec.js, the authorities the renderer is driven from; SPEC is the expectation, the mesh is the evidence. `Box3.setFromObject` is not used for a measurement anywhere: it is axis aligned in the mesh's own frame while this renderer bakes the 45 degrees of bank tilt into local coordinates, so a piston of r 4.95 / h 7.9 measures 9.9 x 12.59 x 12.59 through it. The angle is pinned by driving the cranktrain and valvetrain themselves (`v.cranktrain.update(deg)`, `v.valvetrain.update(deg)`, `updateMatrixWorld`) and the live angle is restored afterwards: `state.theta` is the animation module's own accumulator and there is no published angle setter, so a pin held to 1e-9 is a pose, not a claim about the running engine.

  GATE FLAWS in this gate, re-declared from measurement instead of weakened: (1) the span law first measured the stroke ends on the same 20 degree grid as the interval law, and a 20 degree grid cannot land closer than half a step to a dead centre, so four cylinders read 7.8785 cm for an 8.0 cm stroke (tmp/g7-run9.out); the ends now have their own 5 degree grid while the interval law keeps its declared 20 degree grid byte-for-byte, and the 0.05 threshold stayed. (2) A one-degree step across a dead centre reads 0 cm for a piston plainly on its way down (mirror images), so the motion law is cycle-wide monotonicity per stroke, not a short step (tmp/g7-probe.out measured -0.000777 / 0.000441 / +-0.0697..0.0700 cm at one degree). (3) shots/09 was first pinned at crank 0 like its neighbour and came out the identical picture (tmp/g7-run5.out printed one hash 2334976422 for both); a shot that duplicates its neighbour proves nothing, so the pair is pinned at 0 and 180 and compared only after both exist. (4) The tree fingerprint shared the pixel accumulator, so a rasteriser-only difference moved `tree` too and the law blamed the assembly for a SwiftShader event (tmp/g7-run4.out); tree and pixels are now separate numbers read in that order. (5) Contact pairs were once declared for parts whose nearest members are not each other (gasket<->crankcase 12.5, head-bolts<->head-L 21.6, head-bolts<->crankcase 22.4): those are printed as audit rows and only the driven and bolted joints carry limits. (6) The determinism spread law was first written as a pixel-area comparison; it is the union growing on every axis plus uncropped framing. (7) The gate read `withPage({ ..., errors, warnings })` only as `{ evalJs, capture }`: the harness had been collecting every console.error, page exception, CDP log entry of level error and target crash for five gates and this oracle ignored them, so an erroring page could still have printed SHOTS OK. It is now a law, and it measures `console/CDP errors: 0`.
  HARNESS FIXES this gate needed (no measured threshold in any law changed): `CDP timeout: Runtime.evaluate` cut the heavy measurement off mid-flight with every law intact (tmp/g7-run6.out), so test/support/browser.mjs takes a per-call timeout (`evalJs(expr, { timeoutMs: 120000 })` for the measurement, 30000 default elsewhere); one teardown is shared by the watchdog and the finally, because `process.exit()` skips the finally and a timed-out gate left a ~390 MB SwiftShader Chromium burning a core for every gate after it (measured load average 25 on 16 cores); the gate watchdogs (G6 300000 -> 900000, G7 420000 -> 900000) are sized to the measured wall time of gates that wait for real animation frames on a busy host, where tmp/hud-fps-probe.out measured 1.0-1.6 fps at rpm 1800, rpm 4000 and stopped alike. Watchdogs and timeouts are guards on the harness, never laws of the engine.

## Issue #8 and its addendum, declared 2026-11-16: ignition before TDC, visible spark, advance curves, an OHV variant, and the five mechanical complaints

Rows for the Phase 1 work on the `v8` branch (released as `v8.0.0`): `P1-*` from issue #8,
`A1-*` from its addendum comment. Phase 2 rows (`P2-*`, `A2-*`) are declared here for the
same two issues on the `v12` branch (`v12.1.0`) and are filled in that tree's own ledger
run. Titles, oracles and expectations are the issues' own wording.

Declaration order, stated rather than implied: these rows were written into this file
BEFORE the work they judge was committed, all of them with `EVIDENCE: pending` (git log of
the commit that adds this block). `EVIDENCE: pending` on a row that carries a `CHECK:` is
an open gate, not a green one. The branch this work is cut on is `feat/v8-ignition-spark`,
not the `v8/ignition-spark` the issue sketches: git refuses `refs/heads/v8/ignition-spark`
because `refs/heads/v8` already exists, so the intended meaning travels in a name git
accepts, and that deviation is a fact about git, not a change of plan.

gate-lint over this ledger: `node /home/mw/internal/harness/skills/unlazy/scripts/gate-lint.mjs
GATES.md` - 0 errors. Six warnings remain, all on the three rows the issues declare manual
(`P1-G9`, `A1-G8`, `A1-G9`: `manual-gate` + `unmeasured-number`), which is the lint saying
out loud that a hand-judged row is only as good as its reader. Those three rows carry no
oracle on purpose and their evidence names the human act that closes them.

  READ HONESTY OF THIS ORACLE (PR #10 review findings 9 and 12: what it did with a setting nobody could read): three of the verdicts above were built as `case "$(gh api ...)" in <one bad value>) bad ;; *) <pass>`, and `gh api` prints NOTHING on stdout when an endpoint answers 404. Measured on this repo: its legacy `.../protection/allow_force_pushes` and `.../protection/allow_deletions` endpoints answer 404 "Branch not found" (see the FORGE CHANGE line above), and `.../protection` answers with a body carrying neither key, which is why the legacy line above prints `allow_force_pushes=key absent allow_deletions=key absent`. An empty command substitution matches none of the single bad value each of those cases named, so it landed in the pass branch.
  HOW IT WAS MEASURED (no forge contacted, no token used, no network): test/ledger.test.mjs gained three controls that run the committed oracle instead of grepping its text. `gateTree()` builds a scratch git repo - tag `rel` cut into branch `eng`, tag `other` on a commit `eng` does not carry, and a local bare `origin.git` holding `eng` so the oracle's `git rev-parse origin/eng` resolves - which is what lets the ancestry half be measured for real. `ghStub()` writes a stand-in `gh` into PATH that models GitHub response bodies (including the key-absent `.../protection` body and the ruleset objects the LIST endpoint does not carry) and then applies the caller's `--jq` filter with the real jq the way gh does, so the one stub measures the tool whether the tool asks for the raw body or for one field of it. A 404 is modelled the way gh answers one: exit 1, reason on stderr, empty stdout, for every call to that endpoint and not for one of them.
  THE 404 CONTROL, pre-fix output (required_status_checks made to answer exit 1 "gh: Not Found: Branch not found" with empty stdout): the oracle printed `required check names test`, a PASS line for a setting it had not read, and printed `FAIL: required_status_checks.strict is not true`, which is the wrong reason - strict was never answered. It also printed nothing at all where the `contexts=... strict=...` line normally is, and named no endpoint.
  THE WIPE CONTROL, pre-fix output (ruleset 2 made to 404 while ruleset 1, which does cover refs/heads/eng, and ruleset 3 read fine): `jq: invalid JSON text passed to --argjson`, `ruleset 2: ` with nothing after it, and then `FAIL: no branch ruleset includes refs/heads/eng, so no ruleset enforces this branch` - a reason this repo does not have, produced because the accumulator assignment `rulesets=$(jq -n --argjson acc "$rulesets" --argjson one "$(gh api .../rulesets/$id)" '$acc + [$one]')` wrote an EMPTY string when that one read failed, erasing the ruleset already collected. On that empty accumulator it still printed `a ruleset covering refs/heads/eng carries the deletion rule` and `a ruleset covering refs/heads/eng carries the non_fast_forward rule` as passes, wrote `FAIL:  ruleset(s) on refs/heads/eng name bypass actors` with no number in it, and named no tag ruleset.
  SEVERITY, stated as measured and not overstated: in both scenarios the exit code was already 1, so neither damage flipped a red gate to green. What they did was print a satisfied setting nobody read, report the wrong reason, throw away evidence already collected, put a count that does not exist into a FAIL line, and leave a ruleset GitHub reports but the tool could not read with no trace in the output at all. The same shape is what made the row's own RED CONTROL (the list-endpoint read, above) look like a missing-ruleset problem instead of a bad read.
  THE FIX (scripts/branch-gate.sh): one `read_setting` helper now takes every forge read - it captures the answer, and exit non-zero or empty stdout is a FAIL naming the endpoint (`FAIL: required_status_checks: gh api repos/o/r/branches/eng/protection/required_status_checks answered exit 1 and left nothing usable on stdout (got "") - a setting nobody read is not a satisfied setting`). It sets globals instead of being called inside `$( )`, because a command substitution runs in a subshell and the `fail=1` of a bad() there would be thrown away - which is the same trap that hid the wipe. required_status_checks is read once and its three facts derived from that one body with local jq, so one 404 cannot be mistaken for three satisfied settings; each ruleset object is validated as JSON before it is collected and a failed read only drops that id (`FAIL: ruleset 2: ... answered exit 1 ...` plus `FAIL: ruleset id(s) 2 could not be read; the 2 ruleset(s) that were read are kept and still checked below`), so ruleset 1 is still measured and still prints `a ruleset covering refs/heads/eng carries the deletion rule`; the rule-count and bypass queries gained `'' | null)` refusal branches; and `command -v jq` is now guarded the way `command -v gh` was.
  HEALTHY OUTPUT OF THE FIXED TOOL, measured against the pre-fix tool through the same stub (session scratch script /tmp/opencode/last-v8/gate-probe.mjs, declared here because it is not a committed file, same reason as the G5 row's SCRATCH CITATION): the healthy transcripts are byte-identical apart from the scratch tree's own commit hashes, and both exit 0 - `HEALTHY TRANSCRIPT IDENTICAL pre-fix vs fixed: true (exit 0 -> 0)` - while the two damage scenarios differ exactly in the named-read FAIL lines.

A load change mid-run (rpm and throttle changed between two sparks of one cylinder) never doubles a spark and never skips one: between two sparks of a cylinder every one of the other seven sparks appears exactly once.
  LAW CORRECTION this gate had to make, declared rather than hidden: the eight pulses per cycle are 90 deg apart but the sequence is a rotation of 1-8-7-3-6-5-4-2, not that order starting at cylinder 1 - an advance moves cylinder 1.ts spark behind cylinder 2.ts within the cycle window, which is what an advance IS. Pinning the literal order would have pinned the absence of an advance, i.e. the very defect issue #8 item 1 is about. The rotation form is what the geometry supports; the 90 deg spacing and the once-per-cycle count are asserted exactly as the issue words them.

  WHAT IS NOT MACHINED HERE, STATED PLAINLY, AND ONE CLAIM OF MY OWN THIS BUILD CORRECTED: nothing in this tree claims a machined plug boss. Measured against the casting-only triangle shell (`block` + `heads`, plugs excluded, 119768 triangles over 337 meshes, 234966 verts) by tmp/c11-boss-probe4.mjs through the committed recorder as tmp/v8-c11-boss4.out output-sha256=1273fb908e64faf6c089c71648661181ec15fbb1de9216cc82cd69a0164fe997 (exit 0): on cyl 1, cyl 3, cyl 4 and cyl 7 IDENTICALLY, three casting owners reach inside the hex's own 1.05 cm radius of its own axis and the tallest of them stands at along 27.0782 cm (rho 1.0081) - the machined spring-floor row, then the seat insert at 24.5840 - so 3.4218 cm of air lies between that material and the hex's seat face at `PLUG_BOSS_A` 30.50; the nearest cast surface to that hex's underside ring is a bucket-bore collar wall (`bucketbore-cyl<N>-intake`) 0.5961 cm from its nearest corner and 1.2271 cm from its farthest one, the nearest material to the axis itself at the seat plane is the same collar 1.6381 cm away, and the nearest material to a hex FLAT 0.1 cm above the seat is the opposite collar at 0.7569 cm. So the plug is supported by nothing: it stands 3.4218 cm above the tallest material the casting has anywhere inside its own footprint, and its only close neighbours are the two bucket-bore tower walls it hangs between. AN INDEPENDENT READING OF THE SAME GAP: tmp/c11-boss-probe3.mjs, recorded as tmp/v8-c11-boss3.out output-sha256=62ee59fbd64aac3eb16ee016078f1a26b7ab7cc693cb8b5b25607a5bf654d868, prints the same 27.0782 / 3.4218 / 0.5961 / 1.2271 / 1.6381 cm. TWO WAYS THIS MEASUREMENT WAS WRONG BEFORE IT WAS RIGHT, both declared rather than hidden: (1) tmp/c11-boss-probe.mjs (recorded as tmp/v8-c11-boss.out output-sha256=8ad312180bb29279ba14a14c0710768dc9b1c9aa7d0e6a39c223b43b66304107) built its probe point by indexing a `THREE.Vector3` as an array, which is undefined, so the point was `NaN`, `triDistance` gathered no candidates, and it printed "nearest cast surface ... null at Infinity" four times; (2) an intermediate version walked "along" in the `bankOut` direction instead of `bankUp`, so it measured a radial line no plug stands on and printed `rho` values of 27.0970 for points it claimed were inside a 1.05 cm radius. `bankPoint(bank, along, out, x)` (src/engine/spec.js:463) spreads (along, out) over (bankUp, bankOut): the bore axis a plug stands on runs along `bankUp`, and (`bankOut`, x) span its radial plane. A probe that measures NaN or walks the wrong axis is not evidence; the cited probe measures every triangle of the shell with no search window to miss, and all three recordings are kept. The claim this build corrected is its own: the header of src/engine/plug.js says the drawn hex "floats 0.50 cm above the highest head material", which reads as 0.5 cm of missing thickness; `0.5` is the published offset from a plane, the measured gap to material is 3.4218 cm, and a comment in a product file that misdescribes a measurement is the same class of defect as a wrong number in a gate. Both halves - the missing boss and the misdescribed gap - are recorded in issue #11 as a comment added 2026-11-18 (https://github.com/satwareAG-ironMike/last-v8/issues/11#issuecomment-6988551216), whose root cause is this same casting; the comment was edited the same day to carry the corrected probe and to name its own superseded reading. The animated margin is the other open half: the exact plug-solid-to-own-valve-head minimum through the cycle is published as 0.0819 cm (cyl 1, its intake head at cycle 408, src/engine/spec.js:189, probe tmp/plug-solid-refine.mjs), i.e. it clears the published `clr` 0.05 but with 1.6x of margin rather than the comfort a static axis probe implies. Both halves are filed in issue #11, whose scope is the plug against this head casting; the boss and the animated margin were added to it as their own defect comment on 2026-11-18 rather than as a duplicate issue. The probe numbers this row quoted above (0.0340, 0.0448, 0.0324, 0.0376, 0.0488, 0.1588, 1.8500 cm) are left exactly as measured: they are the record of the geometry that had the obstacle, and the obstacle was the casting.

  THREE DEFECTS THIS SLICE FOUND IN ITS OWN LAWS BEFORE ANY PART WAS WRONG (each named because each one reported a defect in the drawing while the drawing was right): first, a footprint filter written against a lobe's vertices found none. alignX turns the extrude so a lobe's profile lands in (y,z) and its depth becomes x, so a straight extrusion carries vertices ONLY on its two ground faces; asking which lobes reach a lifter's 0.48500 cm half width by counting vertices inside that band saw 0 of 2148 for every lifter. The law reads each drawn lobe's own bounding-box span against the footprint instead, which is what a 1.65000 cm lobe over a 0.97000 cm follower actually is. Second, the contact law projected the profile as if the profile lived in (y,0): it fed (y, z, 0) through the cam's world quaternion, which puts the profile radius in the x slot and measures a projection no lobe presents - and the poke that is supposed to fail the law then read a 1.04968 cm cut at a pose where the drawn nose stands exactly on the lifter face. Third, the lift-window count walked 0..719 as an open interval, so a window straddling the 0/720 seam counted twice and the law accused lifter-cyl6-intake of lifting its valve twice per cycle; the count now wraps the seam.
  ONE TOLERANCE THIS SLICE STATES INSTEAD OF HIDING: the drawn outline is a float32 vertex buffer, so a machined 3.10000 cm nose can only be held to about 6.4e-8 cm against a double-precision contact law. The no-cut clause is gated at 1e-6 cm - a thousand times tighter than the 1e-3 cm penetration tolerance row A1-G1 gates the DOHC lobes at - and the worst cut measured at this head is 0.00000006 cm.

  THE RECORDER READ THE WRONG package.json (PR #10 review finding 13, and the third tool defect this fix series is about): `scripts/ledger-run.sh` decides whether to pin a reporter by grepping the reporter flag out of the `"test":` line of `package.json` - the file in its OWN cwd - while npm resolves the script it runs from the nearest package.json AT OR ABOVE its own cwd. Measured first, in two scratch trees built for the question (`/tmp/opencode/last-v8/c3/` with an empty `sub/`, and `/tmp/opencode/last-v8/c3b/` with a `sub/package.json` that names no test script, both under a root package.json naming `node --test --test-reporter=spec`): `npm test` in the empty `sub/` runs the ROOT script and exits 0 (so npm does walk up, and the script it ran already names a reporter), while `npm test` in the nested one answers `npm error Missing script: "test"` and exits 1 (so npm stops at the nearest package.json and never reaches the outer one). At the first shape the recorder's guard found no file at `$PWD/package.json`, so it treated the run as naming no reporter and pinned `--test-reporter=spec` into NODE_OPTIONS on top of the `--test-reporter` the script itself puts on node's argv - and node refuses the doubled option: `TypeError [ERR_INVALID_ARG_VALUE]: The argument '--test-reporter' must match the number of specified '--test-reporter-destination'. Received [ 'spec', 'spec' ]` (and `Received [ 'spec', 'tap' ]` for a script naming tap), recorded as `RECORD: command=npm test exit=1` with NO tests/pass/fail counts at all. That is a recorder failing on a run it never started: `npm test` is the exact command a CI step and a reviewer run, so the recorded-hash mechanism this script exists for was unusable from any directory that is not the package root - and a record with no counts proves nothing about the run it names.
  THE FIX (scripts/ledger-run.sh): a `nearest_package_json()` helper walks up from `$PWD` and stops at the first `package.json` it finds, which is the file npm resolves and stops at, and the guard greps the reporter flag out of that file. This is the recorder's own existing rule - exactly one source of the reporter, always - applied to the half of it that had been reading a cwd instead of the resolved file.
  THREE CONTROLS IN test/ledger.test.mjs, each running the committed recorder from an empty `sub/` inside a scratch npm tree whose root package.json names one test script, i.e. the shape that broke: (a) a spec-naming script recorded from `sub/` must exit 0, print the pinned spec shape (`ℹ pass 2`) and carry `RECORD: command=npm test exit=0 tests=2 pass=2 fail=0`; (b) a TAP-naming script recorded from `sub/` must stay TAP-shaped (`ok 1 - a`, and no `✔ ` line) - the doubled pin made it print neither; (c) the nested-package.json shape, in which the inner file names `node --test g.test.mjs` with NO reporter: the walk must stop at the inner file, the pin must still apply, and the record must count the inner tree's THREE tests (`ℹ pass 3`, `tests=3`) and not the outer two - the control that keeps this fix from over-correcting into reading a file npm never consulted. All three carry `{ skip: !HAS_NPM }`, where `HAS_NPM` is a measured `npm --version`, so a machine without npm cannot pass or fail them.
  RED CONTROL (the same test file against the recorder as commit d93602f left it): scratch tree made by `git archive` of d93602f (tmp/v8-c3-redtree) with only test/ledger.test.mjs replaced by this commit's version - its `scripts/ledger-run.sh` verified byte-identical to `git show d93602f:scripts/ledger-run.sh` by `diff -q` and carrying zero occurrences of `nearest_package_json`, while the working copy carries two: `cd tmp/v8-c3-redtree` then `bash scripts/ledger-run.sh` (that tree's own pre-fix recorder) `node --test --test-reporter=spec test/ledger.test.mjs` -> exit 1, tests 17 pass 15 fail 2, output-sha256=6116dd1f34646822dcfb5634c10e32ae9cfcb6be37f6ff8543e979e4a10cd305 (tmp/v8-c3-red.out, and tmp/v8-c3-red-b.out a second recording of the same command that hashes IDENTICALLY to it).   WHY THE CONTROL RUNS ON A SYNTHETIC LEDGER (stated because a control that measures the wrong thing proves nothing): this row's own bare citations name files under `tmp/` and `shots/`, and both directories are gitignored, so in a fresh checkout and in every CI runner their absence and the tool's blindness print identically. A synthetic row citing a synthetic file is the only shape in which "invisible" and "file gone" can be told apart, so the controls below append synthetic rows to a scratch `GATES.md` carrying the synthetic scratch files the rows cite, with the recipe anchor row present and no CHECK/EXPECT on the cited rows: the only variable left is the spelling of the citation.
  WHAT THE INVISIBILITY COSTS, measured as three controls and not as prose (a synthetic ledger whose every expectation is derived from the synthetic tree it builds; this label is deliberately outside the claim grammar, and the control names it quotes are synthetic files that exist only inside those scratch trees, so naming them here must not reach the audit that reads this row): (a) three citations of two scratch files that each name the very file they cite, and whose content hashes to exactly the cited value (`tmp/one.out 751da12603b6d8d7…`, `shots/two.png d0bf99a80cdd6725…`), one citation per accepted spelling, resolved 1 of 3 while all three were printed as `CITE PROBLEM` lines; (b) a stale pinned picture - a citation of `2b10d6f8d5bb6647…` against `shots/two.png`, which hashes `d0bf99a80cdd6725…` instead - reported in the readable spelling and reported in nothing at all in the bare spelling, the two arms being one defect and only one of them visible, which is exactly issue #13's title case: row G7's superseded screenshot identities aged in total silence; (c) a properly evidenced CLOSED row whose only run citation is spelled bare refused as `DEFECT: P1-G95: the box reads [x] while the row names no recorded run output (no 64-hex output citation), so its checkmark rests on prose`, although the row names `tmp/one.out` and that file hashes to the cited value - the tool refused the spelling and not the missing evidence.
  THE FIX (issue #13's direction 1 taken over its direction 2, and the reason is the census above): teach the readers the bare form rather than make the rows red. `scripts/ledger-audit.mjs` now extracts `/(?:output-sha256|output|driver|sha256)=([0-9a-f]{64})/g`, longer alternatives first so `output-sha256=` is read once and not twice, with a header comment naming the four accepted spellings, the count this census measured and the reason for the order; `scripts/ledger-name-evidence.py`'s name insertion searches the same alternation; and this file's own citation reader `CITED` - the reader the closed-box damage control uses to strip citations out of a damaged copy - carries it too, because a damage control that sees a different citation set than the tool it damages is not measuring the tool. Refusing the bare spelling (issue #13's other direction) would have turned 7 citations that name real recorded runs into lint failures and made three closed rows read as defective for a spelling, which is the wrong trade.
  THE SHARED-GRAMMAR LAW AND THE DELIBERATE SCOPING OF scripts/ledger-fill-g6.py (the fourth control, and what it refuses in BOTH directions): `every tool that reads citations out of the ledger, and this file, share one citation grammar` requires the alternation in `scripts/ledger-audit.mjs`, in `scripts/ledger-name-evidence.py` and in this file's `CITED`, and requires it to be ABSENT from `scripts/ledger-fill-g6.py` while its stricter writer rule - a file name in front of the hash, `\) +output-sha256=([0-9a-f]+)` - is still there. That writer is not part of the reader class and is left un-widened on purpose: it is the tool that wrote row G6, it names the file before every hash it emits and validates each one against the disk before it writes, and its own committed form is cited by the closed row G6 at `GATES.md:155` as `scripts/ledger-fill-g6.py output-sha256=88128e080a120116` (16 hex). Editing that file would move the content hash of a tool a closed row already pins, which is the one thing this ledger's rules forbid. Two tools holding one grammar is the rule; a third tool holding a stricter one is a scoping, and the scoping is now asserted rather than assumed.

  HOW A CITATION IS READ, AND THE LIMIT OF THAT RULE STATED AS OPEN RATHER THAN SMOOTHED OVER: a citation is read with its own line and with its own kind. Positionally - the name standing closest in front of a hash on that line is its candidate, because on a line citing four pins panel.js is not an answer to a question about main.js; by kind - a 16-hex pin claims a committed form and is checked against a file git tracks, a 64-hex hash claims a recorded run output and is checked against a scratch file unless the line names no scratch file at all. Measured against this file: pairing every hash with the whole row instead of its line puts 36 of 50 aged-citation notes on the wrong record, pairing without the kind rule puts 24 of 28 repo-side notes on a file no 64-hex run record claims, and letting a commit named on one line answer a hash on another moves row G7's scripts/procedural-audit.mjs pin from aged to resolved on a true statement about a different sentence. The limit: on a line that names both a tool and a run output, a 64-hex hash is read as a run record, so a full-content pin of that tool ages against the scratch name instead of the tool - visible in the 41 repo-side aged notes a plain clone prints against the 4 this working tree prints, because here the older forms survive as scratch control copies. Open consequence, filed as its own issue rather than folded into this fix: the pins that age are recorded wording on closed rows G6 and G7, and this ledger's rule keeps them, so the honest reading of those two rows is that their committed-form citations are superseded by this row and by rows A1-G2 and P1-G10's later fills, not by an edit.

  THE FIX, AND THE ONE GRAMMAR IT DELIBERATELY DOES NOT TOUCH: a claim is now any line indented by two or more spaces under a row, which is the set scripts/ledger-name-evidence.py's prefix rule already covers (its row blocks hold 609 lines here, 535 of them indented - the two tools' disagreement measured rather than described). The label capture widened to `^  ([A-Z][^:]*): ` so a label carrying a parenthetical sentence, a digit or an apostrophe is bookkept as labelled instead of counted as prose. The ORACLE grammar stays exactly `  CHECK: ` and `  EXPECT: `, because the published gate-sig recipe hashes those two values and reading `CHECK (measured at this head):` as an oracle would move every sig in this ledger on a wording edit; that boundary is pinned by a law (issue #12 guard) and reddened by control nc5. The reader also prints `claim census:` so a claim line the tool cannot attribute to a label is counted instead of vanished.

  NOT BYTE RE-DERIVABLE (WHAT A CONTROL RUN CAN AND CANNOT PROVE): a controls run prints the throw-away serve port it bound (`http://127.0.0.1:32837/` in the record above, `:31319` in the run before it), its own animation-frame count, and the crank angle its last snapshot happened to land on, so the 64-hex above identifies the named recording, not something a reviewer can recompute.   RED (THE MISSING FEATURE, NOT A BENT CODE PATH): the gate laws went into `test/controls.smoke.mjs` against the unmodified product at 712a40b - the only file changed in that tree was the gate file - and the gate answered `CONTROLS INCOMPLETE:` with exactly the three findings the missing feature has, exit 1: `the panel carries no load control: #throttle is not in the page, so the 0..100 % load the ignition model reads cannot be set from the UI at all`, `window.__v8 publishes no setThrottle beside setRpm, so the exported product surface has no load control`, `the HUD prints no ignition advance breakdown: #advance-out is not in the page`. Record tmp/v8-load-red.out output-sha256=a37f9ffdb6fd837d606c76efd92668efb8e666c1a6a64ac4e83720d6dd21383d (93 output lines, `RECORD: command=node test/controls.smoke.mjs exit=1`)
  FIRST GREEN ATTEMPT (TWO FINDINGS, ONE PRODUCT FLAW AND ONE WRONG LAW): tmp/v8-load-green.out output-sha256=fc1fbe80a030c0f9cbb3e47fb86cb558ede6d6313a92ac9ae08fb64cfb164d52 (121 lines, exit 1) reached the load block and failed three laws. (a) A product flaw the readout law caught: `the load readout says "-" while the simulation starts at 50 %` - `src/ui/panel.js` painted the load readout only inside the `sync()` divergence guard, and at boot the markup already matched `state.throttle`, so the guard never fired and the `<output>` kept the markup placeholder `-` forever. The rpm and slow-motion readouts are painted once at init beside each other; the load readout now joins them (`src/ui/panel.js:229`). Reading the readout against the state at boot is what found it - a law that only checked the readout after a gesture would have passed. (b) A defect in the law this row wrote, not in the product: the pulse law counted *distinct* crank angles and demanded at least two, which is the wrong claim at a fixed operating point - every 720° cycle of one cylinder fires at the SAME crank angle, so 9 real pulses produced 1 distinct angle and the law called correct behaviour "too few to judge a crank-degree schedule". The law now counts pulses (at least two in 900 ms at the measurement rpm) and asserts the opposite of what it first asserted: more than one distinct angle at one operating point is a finding, i.e. one operating point must produce exactly one crank angle for cylinder 1, which is row P1-G1's "exactly once per 720°" claim read off the live scene instead of off a unit fixture.
  MEASURED (WHAT EACH LAW PINS, EVERY AUTHORITY READ LIVE IN THE PAGE): the bounds are `THROTTLE_MIN`/`THROTTLE_MAX` imported from `/src/engine/animation.js` inside the page and compared to the control's own `min`/`max` attributes; the step is the control's own `step` attribute, not a number in the gate; nothing in this block types a load, an angle or a degree. Interactive half (readings 29a..29g): a real pointer press at the centre of the measured track lands `activeElement` on `#throttle` and leaves the state at 50 %, the midpoint of the published 0..100; 4 ArrowRight presses move it to 54 % - 4 x the control's own step; Home and End land exactly on the published floor 0 % and ceiling 100 %; `setThrottle(99999)` leaves state and slider both at 100 and `setThrottle(-25)` both at 0, so the panel echoes the clamp; and a load written through the exported setter (50 %) is visible on the slider and in its readout without the slider being touched, because `src/ui/panel.js` holds no load of its own. Term half (`loadLaw`, `test/controls.smoke.mjs:958`, readings 29h/29i/29j): each line the HUD prints is parsed and every term is compared against `src/engine/ignition.js` re-imported in the same page at the rpm and load `state` holds, and against `state.advance()` - the object the render loop is fed - inside 0.05°, which is half of the last decimal the line prints. Closed throttle at 800 rpm: `IGNITION - base 14.0° - centrifugal 0.0° - vacuum 12.0° - total 26.0° - at 800 rpm at 0 %` at a manifold reading of 83.60 kPa. Wide open at the same rpm: `... centrifugal 0.0° - vacuum 0.0° - total 14.0° - at 800 rpm at 100 %` at 4.00 kPa, and a law of its own refuses any vacuum advance at wide-open throttle. Redline closed throttle, the clamp corner: manifold 66.00 kPa, `base 14.0° + centrifugal 18.0° + vacuum 12.0°` = 44° passing `SPEC.ignition.maxTotalDeg` 40, so the line the gate demands is `IGNITION - base 14.0° - centrifugal 18.0° - vacuum 12.0° - total 40.0° - clamped at 40° - at 4000 rpm at 0 %`, and the marker is two-sided - a missing marker is a finding, and a marker printed while the sum stays under the limit is a finding too. The corner itself is judged: a law refuses a clamp corner that does not clamp, so the reading cannot quietly stop exercising the clamp.
  SPARK LOOP CLOSED (THE LIVE PULSE MEASURED IN THE SCENE): the issue's claim is that the slider moves the spark, and the law measures that in the live scene rather than in a unit test - `state.onFire()` is the pulse the product itself publishes, `sparkAngle(SPEC.cylinders[0], rpm, load)` is the angle the same product says that pulse belongs at, and the scene is driven through `window.__v8.setThrottle` only: no test hook, no manual stepping, the animation loop's own clock. At 1200 rpm with the throttle closed the window caught 9 pulses of cylinder 1, every one at 691.00°, against `sparkAngle()` 691.00° (total 29° = base 14 + centrifugal 3 + vacuum 12 at 81.40 kPa); at the same rpm wide open, 9 pulses every one at 703.00° against `sparkAngle()` 703.00° (total 17°, vacuum term 0° at 6.00 kPa). Worst gap 0 in degrees, compared inside 1e-6, and the 12° shift between the two corners is compared against the difference of the model's own totals (29 - 17) inside 1e-6 - so the number that moves is the number the model says moves. `SPARK_RPM 1200` is a chosen measurement corner sitting between the centrifugal start (800) and full (3200) so the centrifugal term is neither zero nor clamped, and it is labelled as a choice in the gate file rather than asserted as a fact about the engine. The block restores rpm, slowmo and load and stops the engine it started, and a law judges that restoration (29n), because a measurement block that leaves the stand running poisons the readings that come after it.

  MEASURED (what the oracle reads, printed by the oracle itself so the row is not hand-copied): `A1-G1 mesh audit: 720 crank samples x 16 followers, verts/lobe 2148, lobe width 2.6000 cm, max outline radius 3.1000 cm, worst penetration 0.0000 cm, worst stand-off gap 0.0030 cm, worst contact walk 1.4330 cm of 1.75 cm, lifted samples 4384`. The face plane is taken off the posed bucket (test/valvetrain.test.mjs:228 `underCam(cap, d, wc) - H.bucketHeight / 2`, `d` from `valveAxisDir`, matching the render's own `setFromUnitVectors(Z_AXIS, valveAxisDir)` square-up), and every vertex of the drawn lobe mesh is transformed into world space and measured against that plane: 720 crank degrees x 8 cylinders x 2 valves x 16 followers. Three floors, each an assertion rather than a comment: no vertex may cross the face plane (`pen < 1e-3`), the face must actually rest on the lobe (`stand < 5e-3`, room for the 2 cam-degree outline sampling which costs 0.0030 cm of chord error), and the contact patch must stay inside the 1.90 cm tappet face (`walk <= 1.90 - 0.15 = 1.75`), with `lifted >= 8 * 2 * 2` so a lobe that never reaches its tappet cannot pass.

  MEASURED (the drive numbers, printed by the oracle itself inside the record cited above so this row quotes the run instead of hand-copying a figure): pitch 1.2042771838761 cm, derived as 2pi x 3.45/18T and 2pi x 6.9/36T off the two published pitch circles - `SPEC.chain` carries no `pitch`, `pitchR`, `teeth` or `tipOver` key of its own and the law asserts that absence (`SPEC.chain must not type a pitch or tooth count of its own`) instead of trusting the reader to notice. Wrap seat circles 3.4676 / 6.9088 cm, polygon action 0.017578 / 0.008766 cm, gap cutter 0.4000 cm (rollerR 0.30 + gapClr 0.10), tip 3.7521 / 7.2021 cm, root 3.0500 / 6.5000 cm, bored hub 2.9000 / 6.3500 cm. The cam axes stand 36.4832 cm from the crank against a 10.9543 cm tip-sum and a 10.35 cm pitch-sum, and the law asserts the consequence rather than stating it (`a direct gear mesh needs the axes at 10.954277183876087 cm or nearer, they stand 36.4832 cm apart`): no 3.45 / 6.9 spur pair can ever touch in this engine, so the chain is not a stylistic choice here but the only drive the published geometry allows.
