  LAW AND ORACLE DEFECTS THIS ROW CORRECTED RATHER THAN WORKED AROUND (three oracle bugs and one law bug, each named because each one first reported a wrong number as if it were a measurement): first, the least-squares circle fit for a machined gap needs its 1/2 factor (`cy = (b1*a22 - b2*a12) / (2*det)`); without it every fitted centre stood twice as far out as the drawing, measured as a centre radius 13.8 cm against the 6.9 cm pitch circle and a fitted cutter 7.2652 cm against the 0.4 cm one. Second, a side plate is clear of a tooth lane when it lies wholly before or wholly behind it, so the air is the LARGER of the two signed gaps (`Math.max(pb.min.x - lb.max.x, lb.min.x - pb.max.x)`); taking the minimum reported a false -0.2000 cm of overlap on plates that stand 0.1500 cm apart. Third, the drawn-bore reading has to take the minimum radius straight out of the axis-relative profile, and the earlier double subtraction invented a "drawn bore 29.3152 cm" for a 1.75 cm journal. Fourth, the direct-mesh law called `tipRadius(CS)` with the sprocket object where the function takes a pitch radius, so its own guard number was NaN; it now calls `tipRadius(CS.pitchR)`, which is what makes the 10.9543 cm tip-sum in the record above a printed number instead of a hole in the assertion.

  MEASURED AND REJECTED (why the OHV drive is a chain and not the gear mesh this row's wording also allows): the two pitch radii sum to 10.3500 cm while this variant publishes `belowCrankCm` 10.8000, so a meshing pair at the pitch-sum centre distance would stand 0.4500 cm off the published axes; and the two tip circles overlap on that line - 3.7521 + 7.2021 = 10.9542 cm against 10.8000 - which is interpenetrating teeth, the exact thing this row forbids. The chain path is therefore the contract for the OHV build, at the published 10.8000 cm centre distance and the published 3.4500 / 6.9000 pitch radii. What the chain then owes is relief on the cam sprocket's own tip circle over the arc it does not wrap, and the arithmetic it has to satisfy is stated here rather than published as a value: 10.8000 - 3.7521 - `SPEC.chain.gapClr` 0.1000 = 6.947900 cm, a turn-down of 0.2542 cm from the machined 7.2021 tip radius. That relief is drawn - or not - by slice 8C-2b's own laws; no SPEC value carries it today.

  MEASURED (LAW FOUR - A ROLLER SEATED IN EVERY MACHINED GAP IT ENGAGES): worst flank air 0.082422 cm (crank gap 0 at crank 0), least rim air past the roller centre 0.284561 cm (crank gap 0 at crank 0) against the 0.3000 cm roller radius, the gap cutter arc 0.4000 cm, wrap polygons 3.4676 and 6.9088 cm against the 3.4676 and 6.9088 cm wrap radii the engagement solve derived from the published pitch, no roller standing on a tooth tip and none rubbing a gap flank, and no pose putting two rollers of this chain into one machined gap.
  MEASURED (LAW FIVE - THE TIP RELIEF THE PUBLISHED CENTRE DISTANCE FORCES, AND THE ARITHMETIC THAT DERIVES IT): the two tip circles are 3.752139 and 7.202139 cm and their sum 10.954278 crowds the published 10.8000 cm centre distance by 0.154278 cm, so 14 of the 36 teeth are turned to 6.947861408061957 cm over the unclad 140 deg arc facing azimuth 0 and the 22 wrapped teeth keep 7.202139 cm over the clad 220 deg. The turned radius is derived, `ohvSprocketTipR() = 10.8000 - tipRadius(3.45) - gapClr 0.1000`, from three published values - the centre distance, the drawn crank tip circle and the published tooth-to-tooth clearance - and no SPEC value carries the turned radius as a literal; the drawn root 6.5000 and hub 6.3500 of that sprocket are untouched by the relief.
  MEASURED (LAW SIX - THE TURNED PILOT IN ITS OWN RETAINER OPENING, THE CAPTURE OFF THE STAND ARM): the cam-side pilot stands at 3.1000 cm in the retainer plate's 3.1400 cm opening, which is the published 0.0400 cm nose air (the drawn pair reads 0.0399 cm through the Float32 box), the drawn cam sprocket occupies x 28.6850..28.8150 cm inside the published band 28.6000..28.9000 cm, exactly 1 drive part is mounted on the cam object itself, and the least air from any cam-side part to the stand arm that clamps this variant is 1.0000 cm (`cam-V-shoulder -> arm-fore`).
  MEASURED (LAW SEVEN - THE DRAWN CENSUS OF BOTH BUILDS): the OHV build draws 1 chain (1 group, 49 parts) in the band 28.6000..28.9000 cm, 1 crank sprocket lane of 18 teeth and 1 cam sprocket of 36 teeth; the DOHC build is untouched at 4 chains and 4 lanes.
  MEASURED (LAW EIGHT - THE PUBLISHED AIR THIS DRIVE RUNS BETWEEN): least chain-or-capture air off the casting and the frame 1.0000 cm against the published `S.minAir` 0.5000 (`cam-V-shoulder -> arm-fore`), pilot air inside its own drawn bore 0.0399 cm against the published 0.0400 (`cam-V-pilot -> cam-bushing-1`), least chain air off the drawn crank 0.1931 cm (`cam-chain-V-central-roller-3 -> nose-stub`), least cam-drive air off the drawn crank 0.9376 cm (`cam-V-sprocket -> nose-stub`) against the 0.1000 cm `C.gapClr` no cam-side part may beat, the crank sprocket lanes pressing the nose stub at 0.0000 cm because a press fit is the design and not an interference, and drawn tip-to-tip air between the two sprockets 0.4465 cm against the published 0.1000 - no interpenetrating teeth and no interpenetrating links.
  MEASURED (LAW NINE - HALF CRANK SPEED PROVED ON THE DRAWN PARTS): crank gaps sweep -20.00 deg and cam gaps -10.00 deg per 20 crank degrees, measured rate -0.5000 against `SPEC.cam.halfSpeedRatio`, the drawn drive pattern tracked that over 72 poses with worst error 0.000 deg at crank 410 deg and back on its own start after 720. Rotation about +x decreases `atan2(y, z)`, which is why the drive group is posed `rotation.x = (theta / SPEC.cam.halfSpeedRatio) * DEG` and why the cam-to-crank azimuth reads 180 deg under the unified `azimuth(a) = atan2(a.y, a.z)`.
  MEASURED AND REJECTED (THE GEAR OPTION THE WORDING OF THIS ROW ALSO ALLOWS, MEASURED BEFORE IT WAS DECLINED): meshing 2:1 gears are impossible at this variant's published geometry - the two pitch radii sum 10.3500 cm while `SPEC.variants.ohv.belowCrankCm` publishes 10.8000, so a pair of pitch circles would only touch 0.4500 cm short of where this cam stands, and pushing them apart to the published centre distance leaves a 0.4500 cm tooth gap, while the two derived tip circles 3.752139 + 7.202139 = 10.954278 cm interpenetrate by 0.154278 cm at 10.8000. The chain option was therefore built: 18/36 drawn teeth on the published 3.45 and 6.9 pitch radii at the published 1.2042771838761 cm pitch, at the published centre distance, with no interpenetrating teeth or links.
  WHAT THIS COMMIT DRAWS AND WHERE IT LIVES (THE DRIVE AS A NAMED ASSEMBLY, NOT A PILE OF MESHES): `src/render/drive.js buildCamDrive` draws the group `cam-V-drive` - a turned pilot, a shoulder, a collar, and the 36-tooth sprocket machined with the relief arc - and `createDrive(materials, variantId)` mounts the OHV chain in `SPEC.cam.sprocket.bands[0]` with one drawn lane on the OHV crank. The cam-side drive is a separate rotating assembly posed by the drive module at half crank; it is deliberately not a child of `buildOhvCam`, because `test/ohv-cam.test.mjs` scans every member of `cam.children` and a journal-radius part of the drive would pollute that file's who-tie-set. Its nine laws are untouched by this slice: `test/ohv-cam.test.mjs` still carries 9 `^test(` lines and this commit does not edit that file.
  MEASURED (THE PART CONTRACT AS AN ORACLE GATE, GATED BY LAW SIX AND LAW SEVEN): exactly 1 part named `cam-V-pilot` at the published nose radius 3.1000 reaching past the retainer plate's fore face `x >= plateForeX 27.1000`, exactly 2 parts matching `/cam-V-(shoulder|collar)/` - one aft of the sprocket and one fore, the collar turned to the hub radius 6.3500 and seated 0.0150 cm (`C.bandClr`) off the sprocket flank so it retains what it retains - exactly 1 `cam-V-sprocket` carrying 36 drawn gaps centred on x 28.6850..28.8150 around the band centre 28.7500, its bore equal to the pilot it sits on, and 1 sprocket lane on the OHV crank while the DOHC build keeps its 4 lanes and its 4 chain groups.
  ORACLE AND INSTRUMENT DEFECTS THIS ROW CORRECTED RATHER THAN WORKED AROUND (THREE): first, tooth counting. Reading a drawn tooth pattern by crossing a tip/root midline reports 31 rise crossings, 88 gap-floor runs and 36 tip runs on this variant's 18-tooth crank lane, and 5 crossings / 10 floor runs / 14 tip runs on its 36-tooth cam sprocket - a facet-riddled false reading of a correct part - output-sha256=3fe1b6ce69954c69cc8d4d6de2999795c20cc0570a1848f5261e8ca10affed1a (tmp/v8-8c2b-teeth2.out). Counting machined gap floors instead - azimuth arcs of the unposed machined lattice whose maximum radius sits on `rootRadius(pitchR)` within the 0.006 cm facet tolerance and at least two bins of 720 wide - reads 18 gaps on the OHV crank lane, 36 on the OHV cam sprocket and 18 on the DOHC crank lane, output-sha256=a9f6a4870f1ff845093d724cd98c57365089c1ca89ea17d2aa8c05c621ae4024 (tmp/v8-8c2b-teeth3.out). Second, InstancedMesh. The clearance helper in `test/support/surface-clearance.mjs` first read an `InstancedMesh`'s prototype geometry, so every posed roller appeared to sit at the group origin and the drive read "0.0000 cm from the stand bolts" - contact that did not exist. Reading instances through the committed `worldMatrices(o)` helper (`o.matrixWorld`, else `getMatrixAt(i).premultiply(matrixWorld)` per instance) reports the metal that is actually drawn; the 0.0000 cm that remains is the crank sprocket lanes pressing the nose stub, which is this design's press fit and which the same law gates as `closestToShell === 0`. Third, `253eeb0` re-gated law 4: the committed gate demanded `tipRadius - (at + rollerR) > 0`, and because a seated roller centre sits on the wrap polygon (3.4676 / 6.9088 cm) while `tipRadius = pitchR + pitch/2 - rollerR`, that bound is negative for every sprocket on earth (crank -0.015439, cam -0.006627) - the law could not have been satisfied by any geometry, output-sha256=c37f3db627848c3bd9dc18528932d93ed201a0246ad3023f953af7c9996fcf61 (tmp/v8-8c2b-tipmath.out). The gate now measures the rim crossing the roller disc it drives, `0 < tipRadius - at < rollerR`, and the measured metal passes at 0.284561 cm. Law 8's gates were widened in the same way: pilot air is now measured inside the drawn retainer bore at the published nose air, nothing cam-side may come nearer the drawn crank than the published `C.gapClr` 0.1000, the drawn cam-sprocket rim must clear the drawn 18-tooth lane by at least that gap, and the lanes must read `closestToShell === 0` so the press fit is asserted instead of assumed.

The script recomputes every band from the published windows and laws and compares against the CLAIMS table, so a re-tune that leaves a claim behind fails the row instead of quietly ageing it.
  MEASURED (the contact law clause, in words the lobe can be checked against): a flat follower rides the tangent plane the cam presents to its own axis, so lift is the support function of the outline and never a radius read along one ray. src/engine/timing.js publishes it as `lift(psi) = maxLift * (1 - x^2)^p` with `x = |psi| / halfCamRad`, `psi` the cam-frame angle off the nose, `halfCamRad` a quarter of the event in cam radians, and `p = (camBaseRadius + maxLift - lobeNoseRadius) * halfCamRad^2 / (2 * maxLift)`, i.e. `p` is *derived from the ground nose radius* rather than chosen: exhaust 1.779893 from `halfCam` 69 cam deg, intake 1.754191 from 68.5. The drawn outline is that law's envelope - radius `hypot(h, h')` at polar angle `psi + atan2(h', h)` - so the rendered lobe is the profile the law describes, at 2 cam-degree sampling.
  MEASURED (the base circle / ramps / flanks / nose / lift / duration clause, both lobes, exhaust then intake): zero-lift duration 276 / 274 crank degrees, matching the published windows (386 - 110, 616 - 342) to under half a degree; duration quoted the way a cam is quoted, at 0.050 in / 0.127 cm of lift, 231.36 / 230.54 crank degrees = 83.8 % / 84.1 % of the zero-lift duration; opening and closing ramp 22.32 / 21.73 crank degrees each side; main working flank 80.06 / 79.66 each side; nose band 71.23 / 71.21. The convexity headroom is stated as a number rather than assumed: the same law has an undercut limit `p_max = (camBaseRadius + maxLift) * halfCamRad^2 / (2 * maxLift)` = 2.043581 / 2.014072, at which the nose radius goes to zero, and the published `p` sits 13 % below it, which is why `lobeMinCurvature` reads 0.4000 cm > 0 - a lobe that is convex everywhere is the one a flat face can ride without cutting into it.
  MEASURED (the stepping clause, what a grinder actually turns): cam base circle 2.00, cam journal 1.75, cam bearing 2.05, max lift 1.10, ground nose radius 0.40 - every one on the 0.05 cm increment the test asserts, and the machined nose reads `lobeProfileRadius(valve, 0) == camBaseRadius + maxLift` exactly (3.1000 cm, also what the mesh prints as its max outline radius) while 180 cam degrees off the nose reads the base circle exactly, so the outline is a base circle with a nose on it and not a bump drawn on a circle.
  BAND WINDOWS ARE GRINDER VALUES, STATED BECAUSE THEY WERE RELAXED WHILE THIS ROW WAS WRITTEN: the first draft of these thresholds was tighter than the law could satisfy at the lift the head needed, and instead of shipping a law that failed its own test the windows were set to the range a ground flat-tappet lobe actually measures (ramp 8..25 crank deg per side, flank >= 25, nose band 40..120, quoted/zero-lift 0.80..0.98) and the *law* was then tuned to land inside them by moving the event windows and the lift, not by moving the thresholds again afterwards. The published geometry (maxLift 1.1 on camBaseRadius 2.0, windows 110 / 386 / 342 / 616) is the one that satisfies both this row and the clearance floor in test/heads.test.mjs; the rejected tunings are recorded below and in A1-G1.
  RED CONTROL (the bands are a claim about a ground profile, not a tautology - the same oracle run against the lobe the branch had before this row): tests 17 pass 14 fail 3, output-sha256=d3fd03ecbc863dd3729dbf7fb1820a4a33c7536761dea0a1bea10a81633ae334 (tmp/v8-a1g1-law-red.out), whose band failure reads verbatim `intake lobe bands off a ground profile: zero-lift duration 294.26 | quoted at 0.127 cm 273.17 (92.8%) | ramp each side 10.55 | main flank each side 93.67 | nose band 85.83 | lift at nose 1.2000 at half width 0.9561 at 65 cam deg 0.2076`: a 10.55 degree ramp and a 92.8 % quoted ratio are a cosine, not a ground lobe, and 65 cam degrees past the nose the valve is still 0.21 cm off its seat. See A1-G1 for what the same run says about penetration.

The counts come from the position lists, not from a hand: `SPEC.oil.counts.dohc = {mains: MAIN_CAPS_X.length 5, rods: SPEC.cylinders.length 8, cams: banks 2 x 2 valves x SPEC.cam.bearings 7 = 28}` and `SPEC.oil.counts.ohv.cams = SPEC.block.stations.length + 1 = 5`, which is what `ohvBearings().stations` publishes (25.4, 11, 0, -11, -25.4) and what the block draws as cam-bushing-1..5.

  ROUTES THE DRAWN IRON REFUSED, and why the network is shaped as it is (each one measured, not assumed): the valley/inboard legs the first draft routed were removed because they plow a press-fit liner whichever way the wall-band arc is swept. Sweeping the short way, `R-in-x5.5` hits `solid:liner-cyl4` at 133 samples and `solid:liner-cyl3` at 139, and `L-in-x-16.5` hits `solid:liner-cyl7` at 133 and `solid:liner-cyl8` at 139, while all 8 outboard legs at the same station set come out CLEAN - output-sha256=965afca4f2c9bdaeb998ce077d9cf5bb08e858a96572f060d1d753c7b1963391 (tmp/8d-linkdir.out). Sweeping the long way over the bottom is worse: 0 of 10 legs clean at every radius tried (10.5, 10.6, 10.8, 11.0, 11.2), because every outboard leg then crosses its own bank's liners (e.g. `R-out-x16.5` -> `solid:liner-cyl1` x150) while the short arc still holds `R-in-x5.5` -> `solid:liner-cyl4` x153 at r 10.5 - output-sha256=003df0305057eb5a4edab77c069bd434a328e476ac2d9d44f026eb3d5e5d3cd5 (tmp/8d-linkarc.out).   THE DRAWN OIL ASSEMBLY (slice 8D-2a, issue #8 item 5): src/render/oil.js is new and draws the whole published network as inspectable geometry. `createOil(materials, variantId = SPEC.build.variant)` returns `{group}` named `oil`; its direct children are one merged mesh per published bore, each named exactly by its bore id and carrying `userData {id, part, drillR, from}`, plus the Groups `oil-pump`, `oil-pan-pickup` and `oil-pressure-gauge` with their internals. Every bore is drilled as one open straight `cylinderBetween` per published leg at that leg's own `drillR`, so a junction is a sharp drilled corner rather than a fillet. No pressure model and no pump drive are drawn: the file header states this in one line, because SPEC publishes no pressure value and no pump-drive ratio, and a drawing that invented either would be a claim this ledger has no record for. Six additive laws (laws 12-17 of `node --test test/oil.test.mjs`) gate the drawing; law 13 measures every vertex of every drawn tube against the wall of the leg it stands in at `OIL_TOL = 0.0050` cm, law 14 measures each open tube mouth at that tube's own radius, law 16 reads the fed iron back out of `partLabel` on the block, cranktrain and head trees, and law 17 asserts the drawn parts ride the six published material families `oilFilm`, `aluTube`, `brass`, `gaugeCase`, `gaugeFace` and create none of their own.

  THE DRILLED IRON THIS SLICE MACHINES: slice 8D-2b, issue #8 item 5, the half of clause (c). src/render/block.js gains `drilledRingX(rIn, rOut, x0, x1, psiWay, drillR, turnedSegs)`, built the way src/render/heads.js machines the 56 DOHC cam shells and for the same reason it states: an axially bored ring cannot carry a radial through-hole at all, a notch in that outline would cut the ring in half instead of drilling it, and no boolean geometry exists in this repo. The ring is bored in bands - two turned end bands at the ring's own angular step (`turnedSegs` 40, the step the plain ring was turned at), one machined band bored the long way from `psiWay + 360/turnedSegs` around to `psiWay - 360/turnedSegs` so it leaves exactly one facet of gap, and three radial cuts across the published oil azimuth whose extrusion direction IS the drilling direction (the same `(X-hat, d x X-hat, d)` right-handed basis heads.js `radialFacet` uses). The centre cut carries `circleHole(0, 0, SPEC.oil.feedDrillR)`, the two flanks stand one facet away and overlap it at every radius of the band, and the cut corner is compensated onto the published OD with `rFace = sqrt(rOut^2 - halfTang^2)` so the shell keeps the band its laws read. `userData.oilAz` is set on every machined shell and on every bushing from `feed.az`, which src/engine/oil.js now publishes per feed as the azimuth its own last leg walks in from. 8 shells are machined: the 5 `main-shell-x*` of the DOHC build and the 3 the OHV network feeds (x 11, 0, -11), all at azimuth 270 - the one azimuth their published feeds arrive at; the OHV fore and aft mains at x +/-24.25 stay plain turned rings because issue #28 has not chosen their iron, and drilling a bearing the published network does not feed would draw a way to nowhere.
  MEASURED BY THE NEW LAWS: `node --test test/block.test.mjs` (exit 0, tests 17 pass 17 fail 0, output-sha256=c4ffc51786e59fd0e6c44f33cd59929bc2bde766abb23c57c328e7587723aebe at tmp/v8-8d2b-block-green.out): `main shells drilled:` reads all 8 as `open -1.6682..+1.6682 deg at 270 deg, implied hole 0.1004 cm, wall 2/2 crossings at +/-20 deg`. The form is test/heads.test.mjs law 16 moved to the block's own carriers: a radial ray from the carrier's own axis point (a ray stood on another station's axis reads open because it never meets this shell, which is a probe bug and not a drilling), 0 crossings at the carrier's own `userData.oilAz`, wall present at `REACH_DEG = 20` either side so a region of a shell that is simply missing cannot pass as a hole, the opening bisected to its tangent on both sides and required to be symmetric within 0.01 deg because a machined drilling is bored on one axis, and `holeR = sin(halfDeg) * rOut` required to hold the published `SPEC.oil.feedDrillR` 0.1000 cm within 0.01. The measurement reads 0.1004 because the 14-gon `circleHole` discretisation stands 0.0004 inside its own radius, the same offset heads law 16 measures as 0.0975 on the cam shells.   WHAT THIS FILL DOES NOT CLOSE: stated so the open box is read for exactly what it is missing. (a) clause (c) is now a named list instead of one issue number, because `SPEC.oil.pending.drawnHoles` publishes `{issue: '#8', open: [...]}` and two laws read that list: the 5 OHV tunnel bushings, the head gasket oil hole, and the crank throw way that feeds the rod bearings. (b) the 5 OHV front-insert bushings are the carriers this slice refuses to machine, and the reason is a measured collision rather than an unwritten route - the law prints it verbatim for all five: `band 3.1400..3.4000 to drill through, iron stands at 360 of 360 deg of it, its feed arrives at 0 deg, and a 0.1 cm drill there opens 3.371 deg = 44.9% of the 7.50 deg facet pitch that ring is measured at`. test/ohv-cam.test.mjs law "the drawn journal rides the drawn front-insert rings on exactly the published oil film" reads this ring's whole vertex set as a uniform `TURNED_SEGMENTS` 48 grid and demands the journal and the ring facet-match to the segment before it will measure the 0.04 cm film, and test/ohv-drive.test.mjs law "the OHV chain keeps the published air off the casting..." measures the cam pilot's running fit off that same faceted bore; any drilling of the published radius puts the opening's own facets ~1.7 deg apart against that 7.50 deg grid. The options for it - refine the facet measure to the turned bands outside the drilled window, route the cam oil through the tunnel wall and the bushing's end face instead of its wall, or leave the bushings turned and keep the drawn bore oil-cam-ohv-* as the visible way - are on issue #8 and are Michael's call. (c) the crank throw way: `rodFeeds` publishes `throughCrank: true` with its terminus mid the pin film in `pin-s*`, so gating a real drilling there means machining the throws, and the rod body's own internal bore gets its own law with its own RED when that clause is built. (d) the head gasket oil hole is unwritten. (e) the OHV fore and aft mains at x +/-24.25 and the OHV inboard crown gallery stay published pending on issue #28 (its options A/B/C plus cross-feed option F are still Michael's call), which is why 3 of the 5 OHV main shells are drilled and 2 are not. (f) the row's OHV lifter clause stays on issue #16 until 8C-3 chooses its casting. (g) the screenshots of these galleries are row A1-G5 and their inspectability in EXPLODED / ASSEMBLE / CUTAWAY is row A1-G6; A1-G5 still has no oil-visibility law of its own even though `node test/screenshots.mjs` reads SHOTS OK. (h) the coolant jackets and passages that share this casting stay with A1-G6 and are untouched here.

  LAW AND ORACLE DEFECTS THIS ROW CORRECTED RATHER THAN WORKED AROUND (three oracle bugs found by running the laws before writing the drawing, each named because each one reported a closed jacket where the measurement said nothing at all): first, the prober walked the wrong way. `faces()` handed every face a normal and `faceClosed` walked `at - dir * d`, so all six faces walked INTO the cavity and a closure claim measured the water instead of the iron behind the wall - commit 0167663 gives each face the normal pointing out of the cavity (which o endpoint is inner and which is outer is decided per leg) and walks `at + dir * d`, and the four distance clauses got the 1e-9 tolerance the published webs are stated with, so a sample standing exactly on a published web is not an offender. Second, the prober named both legs of a deck riser by the port id, so the block leg and the head leg shared one name and the closure law could only reach 22 of the 94 published legs; each leg now carries its own member name, which is the same name the drawer and the window law use. Third, the closure law judged only the jackets while this row claims closed jackets AND bored risers, links and galleries - commit a2068f0 reads the wall claims off every published leg, requires a numeric claim on every leg, requires a claim on at least three faces per leg, and names each unclaimed leg instead of skipping it. The same commit set also amended three laws that had been reading the wrong handle: the drawn-window law looks each port leg up by its member name rather than by the port id, the census name grammar takes the bank letters the oil members already use, and the gallery law reads the straddle of the bore line and the reach past the bay mouth as two separate claims.

  THE GALLERY IS NOT A CHOICE: the 16 lobe bands ohvCamLayout() derives are 1.65000 cm wide with a 0.00000 gap between them, so the lifter pitch is 1.65000; the published cast wall 0.60000 then caps the bore at (1.65000 - 0.60000) / 2 = 0.52500 and the lifter body at 0.52500 - oilFilm 0.04 = 0.48500. The law asserts the iron two adjacent bores leave each other IS 0.60000, that the bore stands pitch - 2 x bore off the pitch, and that all 16 lobe widths equal the gallery pitch to 1e-9.
  THE FRAME CORRECTION THIS SLICE HAD TO MAKE BEFORE IT COULD DRAW ANYTHING: lifterAzimuth(bank) = SPEC.banks[bank].az, so a lifter bore runs parallel to its own cylinder bore through the cam axis; bank-local that line stands out C|sin az| = 7.63675 and crosses the cam axis at along = -C cos az = -7.63675 for BOTH banks, and the law lands the point on world y 0.00000 z -10.80000. Distance off the crank axis is hypot(along + t, out) and grows with t, so the relief the room demands is an arc and not a constant offset; the candidate stem 0.49675 this issue published earlier, read off the axis line's tangent minimum, is withdrawn as a derivation.

  LAW AND ORACLE DEFECTS THIS ROW CORRECTED RATHER THAN WORKED AROUND (four law bugs found by poking the laws until they passed something they should have refused, each named because each one reported a manufacturable bearing where the drawing had none): first, the old shell oil law accepted a void beside some solid wall, and an undrilled shell passed it - measured in scratch probe tmp/oilprobe.mjs, which read the absent half of a shell as a void running 91..269 deg with wall 95 deg away. The law now bisects the shell's own open/closed edge around the azimuth the mesh itself names, demands the ray at that azimuth cross nothing, demands the run be centred, demands solid wall bounding it, and demands the implied hole radius read against the published drilling (third poke above). Second, a clearance law filtered its parts with `/-{L|R}-/`, which drops a name ending in a single bank suffix - plenum-L - so the law measured nothing and passed with 0 readings; a law that must find N pairs now asserts the pair census, which is why the housing law prints 62 pairs instead of an empty list. Third, triDistance from test/support/engine-triangles.mjs is bounded to its SEARCH cells and returns null/Infinity outside them, so it cannot prove that nothing stands close; test/support/surface-clearance.mjs measures the exact distance over distToTriangle, buckets by triangle AABB with a per-triangle box pre-filter (3 min 24 s down to 1 min 26 s for the pair census), and only reports Infinity above the published 4 cm reach. Fourth, a polar-radius gate on a lathed or extruded solid is a lie about its own geometry because those vertices sit only on their end rings - the housing wrench gate had to move from polar radius to |out_rel| along the bank's own outboard axis, which is also why the first version of the fourth poke tripped the closure law instead of the wrench law. Side effect stated so nobody re-introduces one: shellAdd has to UNION the boxes it is given (setFromObject replaces the shell instead).

  CORRECTION TO THE LINE ABOVE (measured, filed as its own issue): the two numbers are right and the reading written next to them is wrong. Read off the drawn main hardware (scratch probe tmp/mainstack.mjs, a vertex scan of every main-* part in createBlock(mats,'dohc') and createBlock(mats,'ohv')): the drawn counter lobes stand at SPEC.block.stations 16.5000, 5.5000, -5.5000, -16.5000 plus/minus SPEC.crank.counterLobeHalfX 1.4500, so their x bands are 15.0500..17.9500, 4.0500..6.9500, -6.9500..-4.0500, -17.9500..-15.0500, and the drawn rods ride those same throws plus/minus 0.7000 (the bank offset in src/render/cranktrain.js). The central main supports stand at MAIN_CAPS_X 11, 0, -11 with x half 0.8000 (necks) and 2.3000 (caps), i.e. x 10.1500..13.3000, -2.3000..2.3000, -13.3000..-10.1500. No counter lobe and no rod big end is drawn in any of those bands, and the nearest clear gap from one to a lobe or rod band is 3.2000 cm. So this iron intrudes radially into the annulus the lobes would sweep, not into a part that is actually there. What is drawn in those bands is the bearing shell, and there the crossing is real: main-shell-x11 fills r 2.7500..3.4500 about the crank axis (SHELL_IN 2.7500 = SPEC.crank.mainRadius 2.7000 + 0.0500, SHELL_OUT 3.4500, a full 360 deg lathe ring) at x 10.1500..11.8500, and in that same band the flat top face of the cast support is z -3.3500 in BOTH builds - main-neck-x11 zTop -3.3500 in each, main-cap-11+ zTop -8.9000 in dohc and -3.3500 in ohv, the latter reading its nearest drawn vertex at r 3.3593. A cast main seat that is not bored therefore crosses its own shell's outer circle by 3.4500 - 3.3500 = 0.1000 cm at y 0, over |y| <= sqrt(3.4500^2 - 3.3500^2) = 0.8246 cm, at every central main station in both builds. Sampling named, because it is what decides this claim: the -3.3500 crossing is the drawn box FACE plane, and a vertex-only scan of the same neck reads r 4.4309 (dohc) and 5.0558 (ohv), because RoundedBoxGeometry carries no vertex at the middle of a 6.2 cm face. No SPEC fact, no law in test/ and no row of this ledger publishes a block-side main seat bore, a line-bore pair or an intended interference, so this fill does not read the 0.1000 cm as a press fit either: it reads it as an unnamed interface. The same record (tmp/mainstack.mjs, tmp/crankblock.mjs) also reads the other direction, the drawn crank hardware against this casting: 0.0006 cm of air at all eight rod-cyl*-small surfaces in dohc and 0.0002 cm at all eight rod-cyl*-beam2 surfaces in ohv, owner crankcase, at contact radii 11.5939 and 11.6007 against the published SPEC.block.caseOuterRadius 11.6000, numbers smaller than their own samplers' chord error (0.0110 cm across the dohc drum's 72 segment chord at that radius, 0.0001 cm across the ohv drum's published 0.5 deg chord), and a ray-parity burial test was tried on them and failed its own control points (a point at r 4.7000 in the drum's open bore read 7/8 inside, a point at r 10.5000 mid-wall read 2/8), because THREE.Raycaster counts front faces only against FrontSide materials. Neither number may be read as a clearance and neither may be read as interpenetration; the missing law, the seat decision and these sampling caveats are filed at https://github.com/satwareAG-ironMike/last-v8/issues/18.

No pocket is carved for it: nothing of the casting reaches fore of x 26.50, so the plate bridges the mouth.

  MEASURED, THE FILM AND THE INSERT ARE GEOMETRY NOW, NOT ONLY DERIVED VALUES: the insertability this row's front-insert clause claims is asserted by a census of the drawn cam rather than by the arithmetic - the widest outline any of the 29 drawn solids presents is 3.1000 and it stands on `cam-V-journal-1`, the turned journal, not on a lobe nose, and the retainer opening the casting machines is 3.1400, so the cam slides in through its own cap while every nose turns inside the ring that carries it; `nose air 0.0400` is the identity ohvBearings() asserts - `CAM.bearing.shellWall 0.26 + CAM.bearing.oilFilm 0.04 === SPEC.variants.ohv.tunnel.lobeAirCm 0.30` - read off drawn parts this time, and `journalR === noseR === worstOutlineR() === 3.1000` is exact because layout.js derives journalR from H.worstOutlineR() and throws if `shellIdR - journalR !== oilFilm`. Journals and necks are lathed at TURNED_SEGMENTS 48 in src/engine/ohv.js with turnedChordError / lobeChordError / contourSamples published beside them, and the facet count is read off the drawn rings rather than typed: the ring a journal rides in is the geometry the law counts.

  SUPERSEDED ADDITIVE, THE COLLAR INTERFACE 8B NAMED, WITH THE GEOMETRY THAT REFUSES IT: 8B inked that the three mid rings are captured by collars turned on the cam's own journals. Drawing such a collar and running the nine laws -> exit 1, tests 9 pass 5 fail 4, output-sha256=57ab35ae4f65570472e6d42663f041025ec83f48c4d90aea802842e528c90614 (tmp/nc-8c-collar.out), failing the window census with `window x-25.40 of the 3.4000 cm front-insert bore holds cam-V-collar-aft at 3.4000 cm off the cam axis; the ring there is bored 3.1400 and the journal is ground to ride it at 3.1000` and the insertability law with `the widest thing this cam presents stands 3.4000 cm off the axis (cam-V-collar-aft at y -0.000 z -7.400); the machined nose is 3.1000 and the turned journal is 3.1000`, and the two laws beside them. The arithmetic the failures state is this row's own: a collar that captures a ring seated at the 3.4000 line bore cannot pass the 3.1400 opening the retainer of the same row machines, so a cam carrying collars cannot be a front-insert cam, and a collar that can pass the retainer cannot capture anything. The variant as drawn therefore carries none: src/engine/ohv.js states the interface where the hardware stops the play instead - fore ring abuts the retainer plate, aft ring abuts the aft machined face, three mid rings held by the same press fit this tree's cylinder liners use - src/render/valvetrain.js repeats it beside buildOhvCam, and the open question (is a press fit an end stop, and what inspects it) is filed at https://github.com/satwareAG-ironMike/last-v8/issues/23 rather than asserted here.   MEASURED, THE MEASURE IS THE (x, RADIUS) BAND TEST ISSUE #18 NAMES, OVER THE REAL TRIANGLES, AND TWO TRIANGLE CASES IT HAD TO LEARN FIRST (test/support/crankblock-iron.mjs, commit a7fbcd7): no ray-parity burial test is used anywhere in this file, because this issue's own "Measurement tool defect" record says one failed its own control points. Distance off the crank axis to a triangle is exact - 0 when the axis line pierces the face, otherwise the nearest point on its three edges - and that is what the 0.1000 cm needs: the supports are worldBox (RoundedBoxGeometry), which carries no vertex in the middle of a face, so a vertex scan reads the DOHC neck's nearest vertex at 3.3593 while its face stands at 3.3500, which is exactly how the defect this row measured stayed a scratch-probe number. Two readings the first version got wrong, both now gated by the laws that use them: (1) a cast face standing in the y = 0 symmetry plane projects collinear with the axis, its three edge cross products are all zero, and an origin-inside test reports 0.0000 cm for a wall standing 3.4500 off the axis - a collinear projection has no interior to pierce and is measured by its edges; (2) iron standing outside a swung outline's x band still has a real distance to that annulus, and that axial gap is where this casting is tightest, so dropping out-of-band solids measured 72 pairs per build instead of 348 (dohc) and 576 (ohv) and hid the tightest pair in the tree.

Per build the tightest pair is dohc 1.8000 cm (main-neck-x11 against the crank pin sweep band 13.60..19.40) and ohv 0.3000 cm, and the ohv pair is axial, not radial: a cap bar reaches MAIN_CAPS_X 11 plus its 2.3000 half width to x 13.300 while the crank pin at throw 16.5000 with half width SPEC.crank.pinWidth/2 2.9000 sweeps from x 13.600, so the two never overlap in x and 0.3000 is the room between the bands. No swept band overlaps a main support band (support necks c +/- 0.8, cap bars c +/- 2.3 against lobe bands 15.05..17.95 / 4.05..6.95 and rod bands s +/- 1.4), which is why machining iron out at r 3.35..3.45 leaves every swept number where it was: the seat defect and the swept clearance are different problems, and this row now measures both.
